# Native Docs reader — remaining delivery Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task, under the dispatched `ccrc-worker` role. This is one programme with serial work/review runs, not permission for one worker to execute every wave. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Finish native Docs delivery through installed-build verification and an honest handover, preserving the separate operator-only standalone retirement gate.

**Architecture:** Reuse the approved take-up and W5b leaf plans. W6 adds a bounded mockup/asset pipeline separate from the sealed reader; W8b uses the existing history/version transport through the one library adapter and a pure bounded diff. W7 reconciles carries, documents the shipped source, runs independent review and records release/deployed evidence.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript, React 19, Fastify, the existing ccd Python helper, package-local vitest, existing browser harness, `@synapsium-labs/docserver` exactly 1.1.0 for A–D (later extension pins require their own review), existing parse/render and highlight workers. No new runtime dependency is authorised for A–D by this plan; required extensions need their own reviewed dependency and security decisions.

**Spec:** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-07-native-docs-reader-completion-design.md`; base spec `2026-10-01-native-docs-reader-design.md` §5.6–5.8; rich-docs spec `2026-10-12-rich-docs-reader-and-shared-renderer-design.md` G1/G4/G5/G6/G7 and retirement gate. Read the spec with this plan.

**Status:** 2026-11-07 completion plan. No remaining implementation is claimed complete by this document. A uses planned run 1532; later run IDs and deviation blocks are issued at their actual opening, not invented here.

## Global Constraints

- A–D use exactly the released library `v1.1.0` at `50cef012aa671974473d19c2acd77f900e9d03af`; npm integrity is recorded in the take-up plan. Recheck provenance at execution; do not repeat signing/publication approval. This is not the final extension-release pin: any later library change needs its scoped extension design, compatibility checks and reviewed exact-version adoption; F measures the final approved release set.
- Preserve both 2026-11-06 phone blocks byte-for-byte. `E17-READING does-not-hold`, `E17-BLOCKS 2`: production on-request. An old `auto` block is not proof of after-ready or the 1.1.0 build.
- Reader sandbox exactly `allow-scripts`; shell CSP meta exactly `frame-src 'none'`; no same-origin, popup/top-navigation widening, second reader renderer, protocol parser or parent window-message listener.
- Mockups: static sandbox empty, scripted sandbox exactly `allow-scripts`; no MessageChannel/message listener. CSP meta first, click guard second; attribute equals meta. Second load tears down, never loops.
- Existing `ccrc:reader:v1:` storage; separate parse/render and highlight workers; no main-thread highlight fallback.
- HTML at most 2 MiB, above 1 MiB tap-to-render; prescan depth 64 (256 until D-8695 lowered it, 2026-11-16); asset count 64, aggregate 5 MiB, CSS recursion 3, queue concurrency 2. Reader-specific seed limits can be stricter and are not raised.
- Diff inputs at most 20,000 lines each and the existing Docs byte cap; at most 2,000,000 work units (line comparisons plus frontier advances/trace cells) and 512 output ranges/removals; overflow unavailable, no partial result.
- Fixture HOMEs only, poisoned gh where a fixture spawns tools, no live ccd mutation, tmux/registry/unit changes, secret printing or token argv. Fleet changes only through the coordinator API. Refusal is stop/report, not workaround.
- No new worktrees, repository copies, installs or disk-heavy experiments in the coordinator's session. Dispatched children use their own server-created workspace and existing approved install spine only. Mutation scratch is outside the worktree; sequential saved-copy/sha-checked restoration, never git checkout.
- Heavy tests/typecheck/browser/mutations through `ccrc test`, foreground with tool timeout at least 600000 ms. Inside the recorded run use package-local vitest, never bare npx. Each mutation child gets `timeout -k 10 600` (900 for browser) and a measured process cap no greater than 4096; lower it if available headroom requires it. A timeout is not a passing red.
- One task commit after red/green; review fixes one commit per finding. Commit on the workspace branch, never a feature branch. Stage by path; no rebase, force push, update-branch, admin merge, protection/settings change or raw worker clear.
- Allocate and define deviations in the same act at run-open, describe known departures then, carry the issued block in the brief. Workers never guess a number or mint mid-wave. Record unused issued numbers without a definition token.
- Whole changed-file and outward-text privacy scan: actual rc 0, zero hits, including dotted-number shapes. Scan mail/dispatch/PR text before sending, list mail before push and wave-done. No private identities, absolute deployment paths or private security reports in public docs.
- The first rollout containing W5b-1 is **server-first**, whichever tag contains it. Only after both boxes run it does ordinary ordering resume.
- Existing approvals are not re-opened. Coordinator calls below are labelled as such. Reserved operator actions stay reserved; no impersonated ticks or device measurements.
- Required appearance continuity is a final E/F acceptance gate: separately specify, implement and review shared-renderer/consumer support for ccrc's canonical skin/theme catalogue and host-selected visual language. A remains exact 1.1.0; API, catalogue-sharing and compatibility are not approved by this scope record.
- Recorded operator ruling #1418 keeps outside non-repo folders, variants, large files, PDFs and configured tracker links; #1420 requires private draft-review snapshots with a lifecycle. Full parity plus drafts and side-by-side installed evidence gate final E/F acceptance and retirement. Outside folders need not become Git repositories. These are required follow-ons with scoped designs/leaf plans, not Later/Dropped or work silently assigned to A.
- Existing per-read/render safety limits remain until a reviewed parity-extension design replaces them. No unsafe blanket cap increase, unauthenticated working-tree read or sandbox/CSP widening is authorised here.

**Parity inventory reconciliation:** #1418's no-feature-loss promise is not limited to its five named examples. The required follow-on spec must inventory the standalone reader's capabilities against the native reader, including print and figure-overlay Back-to-close, and amend the rich-docs parity table before E closes. Those rows are not automatically waived by their older Later/Dropped labels. Old-origin state currently starts fresh under the rich-docs state contract; reconcile that recorded choice with the later no-feature-loss promise explicitly, without authorising migration here. Any genuine conflict requiring a product waiver goes to the operator before E/F acceptance. Keep the current sandbox/CSP and state boundaries until a reviewed design or explicit ruling resolves the seam; do not add allow-modals or claim a missing capability delivered.

**Recorded draft decision:** #1420's 2026-10-25 operator comment fixes a frozen snapshot at share time, with 'newer draft available' and re-share after later edits; no live reads through the link. Do not re-ask that choice. Capability-link versus signed-in access, reviewer comments, revocation storage/retention and hidden referenced assets still need the scoped threat model/design; the no-session acceptance criterion must be reconciled with the access model, not silently discarded. Raise only genuinely unresolved product choices when their design needs them. Comments, if adopted, never count as merge approval.

## Review Focus

| Failure mode | Expected behaviour | Owning tests |
|---|---|---|
| Abort, body replacement or fallback while history/diff/asset work resolves | No late result applied to another doc, pending fragment preserved | Tasks 6, 12, 13: stale completion, abort and fallback cases |
| Draft asset deleted/withheld/renamed or auth fails | No fallback to committed bytes; visible truthful failure and bounded retry | Tasks 6, 8: verdict table, one relist, 401 sign-in/retry |
| Reconstructed formatting, nested CSS, huge diff | Refuse before expensive work or return unavailable, never partial unsafe output | Tasks 5, 7, 11: pre-parser counter, recursion/budget and diff exhaustion |
| Copied/shared/exported version differs from opened bytes | Preserve pin/ref and disclose unavailable provenance; no active app-origin export | Tasks 4, 9, 12: version links, output structure, invalid `only`/hash |
| A green check skipped the browser, or an old phone block is reused | Exact-tip actual browser/full-suite plus released-build device evidence | Tasks 10, 14, 16, 17: check-step and evidence receipts |

### Common acceptance cycle for implementation tasks

Every task below explicitly calls this cycle with named files/cases. Write its tests first, run the listed command and record the expected assertion failure; implement the declared interface; rerun to green; run related suites; measure each named guard mutation independently; restore/hash-check; commit paths by name with the standing attribution. No tests of mock behaviour substitute for an assertion on real output/DOM/argv. A test already green at baseline is evidence of an existing guard, not a newly measured fix.

Commands shown below are the command bodies passed to the existing `ccrc test` foreground runner where heavy; use the runbook's installed CLI syntax, not an invented wrapper. Unit command template: from the named package, `./node_modules/.bin/vitest run test/<name>.test.ts`. Browser: from pwa, `CCRC_CHROME=<brief chrome> ./node_modules/.bin/vitest run --config vitest.browser.config.ts browser/<name>.browser.test.ts`. Typecheck: each affected package's existing npm typecheck command or local tsc configuration. Record the actual invocation and exit code in task notes.


### Task 6: C1 — one asset plan with explicit pin/failure distinctions

**Create:** `pwa/src/docs/mockup/plan.ts`, `ports.ts`, `pwa/test/mockup-plan.test.ts`.
**Modify:** `pwa/src/docs/DocImage.tsx`, `pwa/src/docs/reader/images.ts` only where shared pin decisions are extracted/reused; `pwa/test/docs-images.test.tsx` if that exact suite exists at dispatch, otherwise add `pwa/test/docs-asset-plan.test.ts` without renaming an existing suite.

**Interfaces:** `MockupContext = {readonly project: string; readonly pin: DocPin; readonly tree: DocsTreeOk}`. `MockupRef = {readonly key: string; readonly raw: string; readonly basePath: string; readonly kind: 'image' | 'css' | 'script' | 'module' | 'font'; readonly depth: number}`. Unique key is the DOM/discovery-order identity, not a URL to fetch directly. `MockupRefVerdict` is the base spec §5.6.3's full union (`inline/kept/external/refused/missing/unsupported/skipped/failed`), copied without collapsing reasons. `MockupSize = {readonly kind: 'listed' | 'class-cap'; readonly bytes: number}`. `MockupAssetPlan = {readonly context: MockupContext; readonly rows: readonly {ref: MockupRef; verdict: MockupRefVerdict; size: MockupSize | null}[]; readonly reservedBytes: number}`. At admission a non-inline row has size null; an inline row has size and its base-union `size` is that admission reservation, not a claimed measurement. Loading may change an admitted row to failed while retaining its non-null reservation basis; reservedBytes never shrinks. The returned assets map contains successful bytes only, and actualBytes counts all completed unique replies, including rejected overflows, independently of admission. This typed basis prevents an unknown historical size collapsing into a tip measurement.

```ts
function planMockupAssets(refs: readonly MockupRef[], ctx: MockupContext): MockupAssetPlan;
function extendMockupAssetPlan(plan: MockupAssetPlan, refs: readonly MockupRef[]): MockupAssetPlan;
interface MockupAssetReader {
  read(pin: DocPin, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<
    | { kind: 'ok'; bytes: Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer> }
    | { kind: 'failed'; failure: string }>;
}
```

The plan retains its immutable context. `extendMockupAssetPlan` uses that same context and the existing rows/reservations: it resolves newly discovered CSS references relative to their parent stylesheet, applies identical listing/pin/MIME/depth rules, deduplicates against already admitted pins (including failed loads), and extends the same nonrecycled count/byte budget. Within one load attempt it never starts a second plan or resets reservations for nested CSS. A permitted whole-view retry is a distinct attempt, defined in Task 8; it discards the old attempt rather than extending or mixing its state. The loader feeds discoveries in deterministic parent-row then CSS-token order, not network completion order, buffering out-of-order completions as needed; concurrency remains two. For references discovered at the same depth from the same parent stylesheet, their token occurrence order within that stylesheet is the tie-break (including import, image and font references); no completion-time or asset-kind reordering. No transport adapter receives admission-policy authority.

The adapter supplies existing Docs loaders/queue/auth; no new HTTP client. The planner calls existing `resolveDocRef`/`imagePinFor` semantics, never a second path grammar. For historical C different from `tree.ref.commit`, the served listing still decides eligibility under W5b, but its size is not C's size: reserve the full existing class cap before fetch, including nested CSS discoveries. Same-pin committed/draft listing sizes may be used only when tied to that exact admitted pin/fingerprint. Reservations never shrink/recycle during that plan, duplicate pins reserve once, and a reservation that would exceed 5 MiB yields skipped/over-budget with zero fetch. The UI discloses conservative historical admission. Amend the base spec §5.6.3 Budgets and its inline size/basis contract to this exact rule in C1; this is the explicitly labelled coordinator design call, not an unrecorded exception to 'all listed sizes'. Claim `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-native-docs-reader-design.md` for that narrow amendment and define the issued departure at C1 run-open.

- [ ] RED table: committed C with modified draft asset reads C; admitted draft modification/addition/untracked/conflict gets DraftPin; deleted/withheld/typechange is missing/refused, no committed fallback; max64 and max5MiB stop before fetch; duplicate reference budgeted once; CSS depth3 admitted/4 unsupported; unknown MIME refused by extension table.
- [ ] Run `mockup-plan` and image-policy suites to assertion-red. Implement the pure deterministic planner with exact-pin listed sizes or historical class-cap reservations. RED historical cases use the same tip listing with both smaller and larger C bytes: admission is identical and never trusts the tip size; two fitting class-cap reservations and the next over-budget row have explicit ordered verdicts, CSS discoveries share the same nonrecycled budget, and no historical metadata endpoint is called. Keep count and byte boundaries inclusive as the base spec defines; reader seed limits remain separate.
- [ ] RED nested admission: extend an already admitted plan and assert `extended.context === initial.context`; use conflicting listing/pin outcomes in two separately constructed contexts to prove each attempt uses its own captured context, not a global current listing. In one attempt, nested discoveries preserve prior rows, failed admitted pins and reservations. Isolate the 64-count boundary with small bytes well below 5 MiB, and the 5 MiB boundary with fewer than 64 rows; each refuses the next nested discovery with zero fetch. A failed admitted pin rediscovered in CSS remains one admitted identity/reservation.
- [ ] GREEN and rows independently removing pin distinction, prefetch size gate, count/total reservation budget, historical class-cap basis (replace it with the misleading tip size), CSS depth and MIME check. Additionally measure every nested-admission row below independently. Assert reader/plain paths use the same decisions, not identical output when their seed limits differ. Commit.

| id | file | one mutation | discriminating RED (prediction, measure at execution) |
|---|---|---|---|
| NDR-C1-T6-N1 | `mockup/plan.ts` | Reset the admitted count when extending for CSS | `mockup-plan`: root plus nested row 65 must be skipped; total bytes remain below the byte cap |
| NDR-C1-T6-N2 | `mockup/plan.ts` | Reset `reservedBytes` when extending for CSS | `mockup-plan`: nested row beyond aggregate 5 MiB must be skipped with fewer than 64 rows |
| NDR-C1-T6-N3 | `mockup/plan.ts` | Exclude failed admitted rows from extension deduplication | `mockup-plan`: failed root pin rediscovered in CSS has one row and one unchanged reservation |

These are implementation obligations, not measured reds. At C1 execution record exact unique old/new snippets, tests, assertion output and restored hashes in the task's machine-readable mutation fence; a compile error, timeout or green row is not a successful guard mutation.

### Task 7: C1 — bounded loading, origins and policy

**Create:** `pwa/src/docs/mockup/load.ts`, `css.ts`, `origins.ts`, `policy.ts`, `pwa/test/mockup-load.test.ts`, `mockup-css.test.ts`, `mockup-policy.test.ts`.

**Interfaces:** `MockupAssetResult = {readonly plan: MockupAssetPlan; readonly actualBytes: number; readonly assets: ReadonlyMap<string, {readonly mime: string; readonly bytes: Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>}>}`; `loadMockupAssets(plan, reader, signal): Promise<MockupAssetResult>` keeps failures in the returned plan. `HttpsOrigin` is an opaque branded string produced only by `parseMockupOrigin(raw: string, ownHostname: string): HttpsOrigin | null` (null means refused origin). `mockupCsp(extra: readonly HttpsOrigin[]): string`. `buildMockupSrcdoc(doc: Document, assets: MockupAssetResult, extra: readonly HttpsOrigin[], scripted: boolean): string` serialises the already parsed document, CSP first and guard second. The DOM extractor in css/load is an adapter, not a claim that DOM code is L1.

- [ ] RED nested discovery: use actual CSS extraction plus loading, not a hand-fed mock discovery list. Root CSS rows P0/P1 both complete under concurrency two; reverse their completion order and assert identical admitted row/pin/reservation order. P0 contains valid `@import` first, then `@font-face { font-family: example; src: url("a.woff2") }`, then `.hero { background-image: url("a.png") }`: font before image at the same depth and parent. A second fixture reverses those two rule kinds. Assert stylesheet token occurrence order in both, so ascending or descending asset-kind sorting fails; import-before-image alone is not a discriminator. Assert exact-pin/listing/draft-withheld outcomes against the captured plan context and `result.plan.context === initial.context`. Duplicate pins across root and CSS load/reserve once even after failure; smaller/failed replies never recycle reservations; the next over-budget discovery has zero reads. Call `extendMockupAssetPlan` and retain its result. A mid-load whole-view relist/context swap is tested on the real viewer in Task 8, not simulated by passing a nonexistent second context to this API.
- [ ] RED loader: concurrency peak2; abort stops new fetches; duplicate loads once; pin movement is returned as its existing explicit failed/moved detail to the view, with zero relists or hidden per-asset retries inside `loadMockupAssets`. Unavailable historical commit fails directly with zero relists and no switch to latest; 401 stays failed/auth, not missing. Actual bytes above the reservation or class cap are failed/size-mismatch and never inlined; actualBytes counts all completed unique replies, including rejected overflow, separately from reservedBytes. Add RED for actual-size overflow, conservative skipped rows and no reuse of smaller/failed replies' reservations. Task 8 owns the one whole-view relist/replan and generation boundary.
- [ ] RED origins/CSS: exact HTTPS only; reject IP, localhost and *.localhost, current hostname at any port, whitespace, semicolon, quotes and wildcard; lexical script-text census never offers truncated origin and never fetches; CSS recursion/cycle and budget failures stay explicit.
- [ ] Run `mockup-load`, `mockup-css`, `mockup-policy` to assertion-red. Implement via the screen's existing queue/loaders and fixed extension MIME. Confirmed origins append only script/style/img/font; never connect/default/worker/frame. No author consent stored across views.
- [ ] GREEN and mutations: concurrency3, hidden per-asset retry, auth→missing, omit actual-size/reservation check, prefix-host accept, same-host-other-port accept, connect-src widening, meta after author bytes, CSP attribute mismatch. Measure every nested-loader row below independently. Commit C1; use Task 10's common PR/review gate before C2 starts.

| id | file | one mutation | discriminating RED (prediction, measure at execution) |
|---|---|---|---|
| NDR-C1-T7-N1 | `mockup/load.ts` | Feed discoveries in reply-completion order instead of parent-row order | `mockup-load`: reversed P0/P1 completion yields the same exact admitted order under a tight budget |
| NDR-C1-T7-N2 | `mockup/css.ts` or its actual ordered-discovery owner | Sort a parent's discoveries by kind instead of token occurrence | `mockup-css`/`mockup-load`: font-before-image and image-before-font fixtures both retain their token order |
| NDR-C1-T7-N3 | `mockup/load.ts` | Forget a failed admitted pin from the load dedupe set | `mockup-load`: a root failure rediscovered in CSS causes exactly one read and one reservation |
| NDR-C1-T7-N4 | `mockup/load.ts` | Subtract unused reserved bytes after a smaller successful reply | `mockup-load`: the next over-budget nested row is still skipped with zero reads |
| NDR-C1-T7-N5 | `mockup/load.ts` | Recycle the reservation after a failed reply | `mockup-load`: the next over-budget nested row is still skipped with zero reads |

For N4 and N5 use separate real CSS-extension/load fixtures with `B = 5 * 1024 * 1024`. Before replies assert the admitted initial reservations total exactly `B - 1024`, with fewer than 64 rows and every individual reservation within its existing class cap. One admitted root image reserves 8192 bytes. In N4 it successfully returns 4096 bytes; in N5 that image fails. Hold a separate admitted stylesheet until that outcome, then release CSS discovering a unique image whose exact-pin listed reservation is 2048 bytes. With the correct nonrecycled budget `(B - 1024) + 2048 > B`, so that child is skipped and has zero reads. N4's wrong recycling frees 4096 bytes and N5's frees 8192, either of which makes the child fit; each respective mutant must therefore cause its own named assertion-red. Filler assets stay individually within existing caps, count stays below 64, and no other failure may be the reason for skipping. Assert the reservation before and after the outcome and the child's verdict/read count, through actual CSS discovery/loading, not a hand-fed list.

Each row changes only the named guard. Record the actual implementation's unique snippet, assertion-red, restore hash and final green; do not label this planned table measured evidence.

### Task 11: D — bounded client diff

**Create:** `pwa/src/docs/reader/diff.ts`, `pwa/test/reader-diff.test.ts`.

**Interfaces:** pure L1, no library/DOM/worker/api imports.

```ts
type ReaderDiff =
  | { kind: 'available'; ranges: readonly (readonly [number, number])[];
      removedAfter: readonly number[] }
  | { kind: 'unavailable'; why: 'bytes' | 'lines' | 'work' | 'output' };
function readerDiff(before: string, after: string): ReaderDiff;
```

Line numbers are 1-based in **after**, ranges inclusive and sorted/nonoverlapping. A deletion is marked after the preceding surviving line; 0 means before the first line. Empty identical strings yield no ranges/removals. Treat CRLF as one separator, retain final-empty-line semantics consistently with `data-line`; a test renders the real parser to hold that mapping. Released 1.1.0's `src/enhance/changes.ts` explicitly accepts removedAfter 0 at the start. Pin that behaviour with the real library, including an empty after document; do not invent a new unsupported-position failure or manufacture a different mark.

- [ ] RED table as assertions: identical→empty; insertion after line1→range[2,2]; deletion before first→removedAfter[0]; replace line2→range[2,2] plus the deletion marker according to the real library rendering contract; CRLF/LF and final newline; Unicode text; 20,001 lines→unavailable lines; exhausted comparisons→unavailable work; >512 output marks→unavailable output. Use 513 isolated single-line insertions separated by unique stable lines; measure that the 2,000,000 work cap is not exhausted so removing the output guard alone yields available and reds this assertion. Keep a separate fixture for work exhaustion.
- [ ] Run `reader-diff`; record failures before implementation. Implement prefix/suffix trimming and budgeted Myers, shared work counter ≤2,000,000 including line comparisons, frontier advances and copied trace cells, no quadratic matrix, no unbounded fallback. Use existing UTF-8 byte measurement/cap, do not enumerate another cap constant.
- [ ] GREEN plus a deterministic reference oracle on small fixtures and symmetry/reconstruction checks (bounded fixed seed). Add mutations removing each bytes/lines/work/output guard and changing line-origin/CRLF semantics. Record finite wall-clock/process bounds without a disk benchmark. Commit.

### Task 12: D — history seed, changes adapter and version state

**Create:** `pwa/src/docs/reader/history.ts` (pure seed/navigation decisions), `pwa/src/docs/readerHistory.ts` (view-owned lifecycle), `pwa/test/reader-history.test.ts`, `reader-history-adapter.test.tsx`, `reader-history-types.test-d.ts`, `pwa/browser/reader-history.browser.test.ts`.

**Interfaces:**

```ts
interface ReaderCommit {
  readonly sha: string; readonly subject: string; readonly date: string;
  readonly parents: readonly string[];
}
interface ReaderVersionSeed {
  readonly sha: string; readonly draft: boolean;
  readonly commits: readonly ReaderCommit[] | null; readonly only: string | null;
}
function readerVersionSeed(sha: string, draft: boolean,
  log: DocsLogOk | null, only: string | null): ReaderVersionSeed | null;
```

Here log null means history unavailable, seed null means invalid full served hash, commits null means no history supplied, only null means no day filter. Types are type-only imports from shared; no library import here. Map `DocsLogEntry.parent` to [] or [parent]; omit author, bound subject to200 UTF-16 units/date64, commits100/parents16. `only` must be a full hash admitted by the supplied entries **or their parent hashes**, otherwise null. The released day menu uses the oldest day's parent as its baseline, which may not itself appear as an entry; retain that valid boundary case. Never silently cut a malformed hash into validity.

Add optional callbacks/seed to `ReaderMountInput`: `version?: ReaderVersionSeed`, `changes?: (sha: string) => Promise<{ranges: readonly (readonly [number, number])[]; removedAfter?: readonly number[]} | null>`, `openVersion?: (v: {sha?: string; only?: string}) => void`. Bridge is the only runtime library host import, type-test both directions against `HostAdapters`. Replace `withServedVersion`'s history stripping only for validated D input; lab mounts remain history-free.

If any prerequisite fails, keep both previews and report pending.
- [ ] Operator sequence: denylist condition; only justified safe root moves, preserving existing Git history and without forcing non-repo folders into Git; remove old `/docs` proxy mapping on every PWA origin **before** any stop; prove native content/ref/draft/auth; update operator preview convention (durable GitHub links unchanged); stop forwarder; stop old docserver, retain history/directory; prove listener closed.
- [ ] Verify outcome without printing private config/secrets. Rollback never serves raw standalone HTML on authenticated app origin. If cutover fails, record exact boundary and keep a safe separately authenticated preview; no hidden infrastructure workaround.
- [ ] With final receipts committed and all required run/item states measured, mail MERGED/closure before closing the last run. Clear coordinator intent only when the role ends. Final response distinguishes native delivery from standalone retirement, with no “complete” claim ahead of evidence.

## Per-wave brief and acceptance contract

Each dispatch includes only wave specifics: programme/spec/plan paths; exact task range and actual base; allowed files and overlapping-claim agreement; produced/consumed interfaces; existing approvals and evidence applicable to that wave; issued deviation block and defined departures; chrome/test routing; required mutation/browser/device rows; branch-discipline sentence; stop conditions. Do not paste the standing worker skill.

**Routing:** main worker Opus/high; native Sonnet/high implementers (including parser/asset/diff/adapter tasks5–7/9/11–12); Sonnet/medium only for mechanical documentation; per-task Opus/high reviewers for untrusted-input/boundary tasks and Sonnet/medium for mechanical documentation tasks. Every Agent call names model and required effort literally; never Fable. Workflow mode off. External reviewer uses the held-out contract in Tasks16/design §8, never the older single-refuter shortcut in historical plan text.

**Acceptance:** remeasure wave-done, server-verify fingerprint, separate review run, accepted report, exact-head required PR/queue checks, actual MERGED. Feature worker does not merge their own PR; coordinator ledger work stays on ledger branches. Open successor before closing predecessor; report copies survive child reclamation. No child wait hidden behind a long coordinator workflow or poll loop.

## Deviations found

- **D-8659 (2026-11-16)** — mockup-historical-class-cap. Task 6 (C1): departs from base §5.6.3's Budgets sentence ("All are decided from listed sizes **before any fetch**") and from the `inline` verdict's `size`, which that sentence makes the listed size. For a historical commit C that differs from the served tree's commit, the served listing still decides eligibility, but its size is not C's size: admission reserves the full existing class cap per asset before any fetch, nested CSS discoveries included, and a reservation never shrinks or recycles within one attempt. An `inline` row's `size` is that reservation. Same-pin listing sizes are used only when tied to the exact admitted pin. The UI discloses the conservative historical admission. This is the coordinator design call the completion plan's Task 6 labels; Task 6 amends the sentences. Issued to run 2209 at its opening.

- **D-8660 (2026-11-16)** — mockup-relist-in-view. Found while executing C1 (Task 7), from run 2209's reserve. Departs from base §5.6.3's Loader bullet, which sits under `mockup/load.ts`: "A `draft-changed`, `worktree-moved`, `worktree-gone` or `unknown-commit` on any asset triggers one re-list and re-plan (§2 (e)). A second such failure renders those rows `failed`, with 'changing, retry'." `loadMockupAssets` returns each such failure as that row's `failed` word and re-lists, re-plans and retries nothing itself; the one whole-view re-list and re-plan is the viewer's (Task 8), as a new attempt with a fresh context and budget; and on a historical commit an `unknown-commit` fails at once, with no re-list (W5b's refinement (p)). Task 7 amends the bullet.

- **D-8661 (2026-11-16)** — mockup-prescan-blocked-pops. Found at C1's Task 5 review, from run 2209's reserve. Departs from base §5.6.2's pre-scan rule: "An end tag pops down through the nearest matching name, and is ignored when no name matches", "It skips nothing. Comments, attribute values and script text count too" (as a rule for pushing and popping), and the formatting multiset "which only each element's own end tag decrements". A real parser ignores some end tags that rule let pop: one with a special element above its match, and one inside a comment, an attribute value or raw text. The pre-scan's depth and formatting could stay small while the parser's stack and list of active formatting elements grew (7 M nodes in parse5 for a page the old rule admitted at formatting 1). The pre-scan still counts every tag everywhere, but a tag pushes or pops only in markup: comments, bogus comments and a tag's attributes are tokenized exactly and push nothing; an end tag pops only when its match is the top of the scan's stack, and `</body>`, `</html>` and `</form>` never pop (top-only replaced the earlier blocker rule after the fifth bypass, operator, ask 452); raw text is read as the tokenizer reads it (`script`'s escape states included) and pushes nothing, its opener's own end tag ending it (refined after a fourth bypass, operator, ask 451); a blocked end tag changes nothing; and a formatting count drops only when its own end tag pops it. Every change refuses more, except that the exactly tokenized regions (comments, bogus comments, attributes and raw text) no longer push. Task 5 amends the sentences.

- **D-8662 (2026-11-16)** — mockup-srcdoc-char-cap. Found at C1's Task 7 review, from run 2209's reserve; the coordinator ruled the cap and its conditions (asks 446 and 447, 2026-11-16). Departs from base §5.6.3's Budgets sentence "The worst srcdoc is then about a 2 MiB page plus 5 MiB of base64 assets, about 9.1 M chars, inside the 11.2 M measured to render (E4)", which made the asset budget alone the page's bound, and from this plan's Task 7 interface `buildMockupSrcdoc(...): string`. A pin is reserved once but inlined at every use, and a stylesheet's data carries its children's again, so 500 uses of a 100 KiB image built a page of 68 285 665 chars (about 68.3 M). `MOCKUP_MAX_SRCDOC_CHARS`, derived from the existing constants as the Budgets worst case (about 9.1 M, inside E4's 11.2 M), now holds the final string: uses are inlined depth-first in document order while the output fits, every later use is `skipped:'over-budget'` and stays as written, and a page whose own serialization, CSP meta and guard included, passes the cap is refused before anything is inlined (`too-large`, Export's word, shown with Download as base §4.17's Export refusal is). `buildMockupSrcdoc` returns `{ok: true, page, plan}` or `{ok: false, why: 'too-large', chars, cap, plan}` instead of a string (the field is `page` because the W8a scan keeps a `srcdoc` name out of `pwa/src/docs` outside its allowances). A stylesheet also inlines its references only within `MOCKUP_MAX_CSS_DEPTH` of use. Task 7 amends base §5.6.2 step 6 and the Budgets sentence.

All of run 2209's reserve is defined: 8660, 8661, 8662.

- **D-8663 (2026-11-16)** — mockup-prescan-foreign-content. Found at C1's second adversarial review of the pre-scan (Results' round 2); the coordinator ruled the shape and allocated the number separately (ask 448, 2026-11-16), and the operator ruled each later step (asks 450 to 454). Departs from base §5.6.2's "It stops as soon as either limit is exceeded", from its rule that every non-void start tag pushes, and from the refusal's union `why: 'too-deep' | 'too-many-tags' | 'too-many-formatting'`, and replaces them. Inside svg `foreignObject`, `desc` or `title`, or MathML `mi`, `mo`, `mn`, `ms`, `mtext` or `annotation-xml`, the parser ignores the integration point's own end tag and the root's while an HTML element is current, so D-8661's pop rule still let the scan pop what the parser kept: a formatting-list leak measured at 26.6 s in Chromium, and a stack about 9 000 deep at 22.3 s. v3 (ask 448): each scan entry carries its namespace, any start tag inside an integration point refuses the page as `foreign-markup` (text there stays admitted), and a raw-named element inside foreign content holds text only (any markup in it but its own end tag and a `<![CDATA[` opener refuses). v4 (ask 450): a self-closed `svg` or `math` pushes nothing. v5 (ask 451): raw text and attributes are read by the exact tokenizer (shared with D-8661). v6 (ask 452): any `<![CDATA[` while a foreign entry is open and any raw-text start tag inside `template` refuse as `foreign-markup`, `</p>`/`</br>` over a foreign top pop to HTML, and `font` breaks out only with `color`, `face` or `size`. v7 (asks 453 and 454), the rule that ships: the foreign entries are one run on top of the stack that mirrors the parser's; any self-closed foreign element pushes nothing; a breakout pops the whole run and is pushed as HTML; `</p>` and `</br>` pop the run and then run as HTML end tags; any other end tag inside foreign content walks down the run to the first entry with its name and pops through it (outside foreign content end tags stay top-only, D-8661); and the page refuses as `foreign-markup` for a root `svg` or `math` with a `template` or `select` open or after a `frameset` start tag, for `</p>` or `</br>` with an integration point open, and for a foreign end tag whose walk reaches an HTML entry or the bottom of the stack. Task 5 amends the sentences; base §5.6.2 and `prescan.ts`'s header state the same rule.

- **D-8671 (2026-11-16)** — mockup-prescan-attribute-cap. Found at C1's seventh adversarial review of the pre-scan; the operator chose the cap (ask 456) and the coordinator allocated the number separately. Departs from base §5.6.2's limit list (`MOCKUP_MAX_DEPTH`, `MOCKUP_MAX_TAGS`, `MOCKUP_MAX_OPEN_FORMATTING`) and the refusal union, and replaces both: the parser copies every attribute into each reconstructed and adoption-agency clone, so a formatting element with 150 000 attributes reconstructed 4 000 times took 19.1 s in Chromium while the scan admitted it at formatting 1. `MOCKUP_MAX_ATTRIBUTES` bounds every start tag's attributes, counted by the exact tokenizer, and a tag past it refuses the page as `too-many-attributes` through the existing pre-scan refusal. The value is measured. It was first 64, where the reconstruction worst case at the cap parsed in 389 ms in Chromium 156 (**local**, one run's reading). It is 32 since fix round 2, lowered together with D-8695's depth and formatting limits after the merge queue's hosted runner read that shape at a 1 410 ms median. What is enforced is the browser leg's 1 000 ms median gate, and committed mockups carry at most 7. Task 5 amends the sentences.
- **D-8695 (2026-11-16)** — mockup-prescan-limits-lowered. Found at C1's fix round 2: merge-queue run 43905778054 dequeued PR #2915 when the browser leg's hosted runner read two gated shapes over the 1 000 ms gate (reconstruction at 32 open formatting elements with 64 attributes each, a 1 410.4 ms median; the text-heavy page at depth 256, 1 051.6 ms). The coordinator ruled that the gate stays and the limits come down (fix-round mail 21828), and allocated the number (ask 457, option 0). The operator's answer to ask 457 supersedes the base spec's share-export decision 9 where it says "`MOCKUP_MAX_DEPTH` stays 256" (base §0's decision log, annotated in place). Departs from base §5.6.2's `MOCKUP_MAX_DEPTH = 256` (and "`MOCKUP_MAX_DEPTH` stays 256"), from the completion spec's §5 Prescan row ("depth limit 256") and its sentence "Depth stays 256.", and from the W6 measurement's `MOCKUP_MAX_OPEN_FORMATTING = 32`, and replaces them: depth is 64 and open formatting 16; `MOCKUP_MAX_ATTRIBUTES` goes from 64 to 32 under D-8671; `MOCKUP_MAX_TAGS` stays 15 000. The values are the smallest measured set with each gated shape at most 150 ms alone on the fleet box (Chromium 156.0.8078.4, `chromium-1248`, headless Linux, load average about 7 to 9; **local** readings): reconstruction 108 ms, text-heavy 138 ms, every other gated shape at most 55 ms. Text-heavy needed depth: 240 ms at 128, 132 ms at 64, and a floor near 130 ms from the 2 MiB size. The committed mockups keep at least 2.7x headroom (depth 17, open formatting 2, attributes 7 and tags 5 394 against 64, 16, 32 and 15 000; tags are the least, at 2.78x). The cost: a page that omits end tags is refused sooner (32 open paragraphs in closed divs, or 21 lists whose items are left open). The 1 000 ms gate is unchanged, and the hosted margin is the coordinator's full CI run's to show. Task 5 amends §5.6.2's constants, its Status and the W6 sentence in place.

- **D-8711 (2026-11-17)** — mockup-view-host-inputs. Found at C2's Task 8 design, from run 2342's reserve. Departs from this plan's Task 8 interface `MockupViewProps = {readonly show: DocsShowOk; readonly context: MockupContext; readonly reader: MockupAssetReader}`: the view also takes three inputs of its leaf that `show`, `context` and `reader` cannot carry. `page` is the leaf's location: Full screen, New tab and Close navigate from it, and its `frame` says whether the view renders inline or at `frame=full` (base §5.6.7); `mockupPage(context)` cannot stand in for it, because it drops a ref view's `ref` and `view`. `relist()` is the visit's tree re-list (`retryTree`), the door for the one automatic whole-view re-list and the manual Retry that base §5.6.3's Loader bullet and D-8660 give the view, since no reader may re-list (D-8660). `download` is the leaf's own Download button: saving the original bytes goes through `downloadDocFile` with the screen's loaders and the leaf's failure words, which none of the three carries (the reader reads admitted asset pins only, and `show` is the page's answer, not a way to save it), so the leaf hands its one Download button in, as it does to the SVG leaf and as the HTML stand-in took it. `MockupFrameProps` is the plan's, exactly: the frame reads its `csp` attribute back from the page's own leading CSP meta, so attribute and meta are equal by construction.
- **D-8712 (2026-11-17)** — mockup-census-row-cap. Found at C2's Task 8 review (Opus · high) and ruled by the coordinator (mail 22476, option (b) of the worker's question), from run 2342's reserve. Departs from base §5.6.7's Census item ("collapsed by default ("Assets and references · 12 loaded · 3 not loaded"), one row per reference showing: ...") and §5.6.6's Links list ("Each `a[href]`/`area[href]` goes through `resolveDocRef`", a row for each): each fold on screen draws at most `MOCKUP_CENSUS_ROW_CAP` (500) rows, then one closed line naming how many more are not listed, and the folds' summary counts stay totals over every reference. The pre-scan bounds tags, not `srcset` candidates, so a hostile page can carry about 100 000 references, and a fold that draws that many rows on one tap breaks §5.6.2's rule that a limit fails safe and never freezes the console. The constant is defined once in `mockup/policy.ts` beside the other mockup limits and pinned at 500. Widened in place at C2's fix round 1 (review 2361 F3, the coordinator's ruling in mail 22714): it also departs from §5.6.9 step 1's "A missing asset becomes a row in the sheet". The Export's list of the assets left out draws at most 500 rows too, then the same closed line, on the sheet and in the saved file. That list holds one label per asset reference that was not written, so a hostile page reaches it the same way, and the saved file's copy is drawn again on every size-fit try. The result keeps every label, and every placeholder stays in the file. The saved Export's links list is not capped: Task 9's final-bytes cap bounds it.

No new deviation number is defined by this documentation-only plan. At each implementation run opening the coordinator allocates and defines its known departures in the ledger in the same act. The historical class-cap asset reservations (including the base spec's listed-size amendment), conditional Docs mount focus in Task 13 (including its three rich-docs focus sentences), conservative diff limits and serial C/D decomposition are labelled coordinator calls, not historical operator rulings. A departure from an existing normative sentence gets an issued entry before dispatch, not a guessed number.

### C1 — run 2209 (the first item and Tasks 5-7), 2026-11-16

Base `7ccd594c3` (main with B2 #2886 and ledger #2893), branch `ws/docs-reader-c1`. Anchors rechecked at the base: `resolveDocRef`, `imagePinFor`, `DocImage.tsx` and `reader/images.ts` were present; `docs-scans.test.ts` held no parser census. That was asked (ask 445) and ruled: create it there as M5.16's DOMParser bullet, with no number. Every figure below is reproduced by a committed test unless it is marked **local**. Commits: F2 `25c04c1ff`, F1 `2fc24589b`, F3 `a527dea94`, Task 6 `8fb17f2cd`, Task 5 `6501a01d3`, Task 7 `2a0c72011`, these Results `2ef6fa23d`, and fix round 1 (below): `0b5e7edc8`, `d121ed9d0`, `bb3097b3c` and its Results commit.

**First item (review run 2208's F1-F3).**
- F2: `reader-frame.test.tsx` passes a version context through `ReaderFrame`'s link context, `onLink` and `routeReaderLink`. A frame `navigate` keeps `at` and `ref`. The row was measured red with the frame's link context set to `pageAt: null` (1 failed | 50 passed; fence below).
- F1: refinement (o) names Q2 (a) and D-8654. Refinement (m)'s identical stale "pending Q1" is fixed in the same commit (Q1 (a), D-8648).
- F3: the round-2 checks ran on those Results and were re-run at the head by review run 2208.
- Sonnet · medium review: no findings.

**Task 5 — the pre-scan and the one parser (D-8661, D-8663, D-8671).**
- `mockup/prescan.ts` (L1) decides, before `mockup/parse.ts` constructs the only `DOMParser` in `pwa/src`, whether the native parse may run.
- Clause C1 of `docs-scans.test.ts` has 37 rows: the allowance, plus one planted row for each term (`parseHTML` and `setHTML` included, the escaped and aliased constructors, a second parser and an alias inside parse.ts, and one in prescan.ts).
- Seven adversarial Opus · high reviews shaped it. From round 2 on, the oracle was real Chromium 156. The coordinator released each bypass to the operator, who ruled each design step.

| Round | Version read | Bypass class | Measured (all **local** except where a committed row now refuses the shape) | Ruling |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | v1 | an end tag the parser ignores still popped and lowered the formatting count | parse5: 7 M nodes; one `</span>` popped 255 divs the parser kept | coordinator: v2, D-8661 |
| 2 | v2 | markup inside svg `foreignObject`/`desc`/`title` or math `mi`: the parser ignores the integration point's and the root's end tags | Chromium 26.6 s (formatting leak), 22.3 s (hidden stack about 9 000 deep) | coordinator ask 448: v3, D-8663 |
| 3 | v3 | a self-closed `<svg/>`/`<math/>` before `object`/`applet`/`marquee`/`template` made the scan read HTML as foreign | 64 KB page admitted at f=1: 1.6 M elements, 57.7 s | operator ask 450: v4 |
| 4 | v4 | a nested raw-text name kept the scan in raw mode; `<svg / >` was taken as self-closed | 818 K elements, 17.2 s; 632 K, 9.5 s | operator ask 451: v5 (exact raw text, exact attribute tokenizer) |
| 5 | v5 | CDATA after leaving foreign content; a raw-text start the parser ignores (`<template><col>`); stale entries after implicit closes (`<p><p>`, `<button><button>`, `<h1><h2>`, the form pointer) | 903 K elements, 18.5-19.1 s; 909 K, 20 s | operator ask 452: v6 (top-only pops; CDATA and template raw starts refuse) |
| 6 | v6 | a foreign end tag Chromium pops through but the scan ignored, then a breakout popping entries Chromium holds as HTML specials (reachable from ordinary `<svg><path/></svg>`) | 55 KB page: 909 K elements, 19.5 s | operator ask 453: v7. Its first draft refused desk-nav-mockup.html (K1) at depth 257, so operator ask 454 chose exact foreign tracking within a provable window, with conditions from the coordinator |
| 7 | v7 | foreign content held (about 75 K differential-fuzz and 29 K reconstruction prefixes, 0 mismatches against Chromium); new class: attribute count (each reconstruction or adoption clone copies every attribute) | 900 KB page admitted at f=1, depth 3: 19.1 s | operator ask 456: an attribute cap, D-8671; no eighth round |

**The final rule (v7 plus the cap).**
- Tags are counted everywhere.
- Comments, bogus comments, attributes and raw text (script's escape states included) are tokenized exactly and push nothing.
- Outside foreign content, an end tag pops only its own top entry; `</form>`, `</body>` and `</html>` never pop.
- Inside svg/math, the scan holds the foreign run exactly as Chromium does and refuses `foreign-markup` wherever it cannot model the parser. That covers any start tag inside an integration point, markup in a foreign raw-named element, CDATA with foreign open, a raw-text start inside `template`, an svg/math root in template/select or after a frameset, and a foreign end tag whose walk reaches HTML.
- Limits at v7 plus the cap (historical): depth 256, tags 15 000, open formatting 32, attributes 64 per start tag. **Current limits** (fix round 2, D-8695 and D-8671): depth 64, tags 15 000, open formatting 16, attributes 32 per start tag.
- **The classes are closed by the rule, not only their instances:**
  - Top-only pops and exact foreign tracking mean the scan never pops an entry Chromium keeps. That is the structural condition the 8-round adoption agency turned into every formatting storm from round 1 to round 6.
  - Every start tag's attributes are capped, which closes round 7's clone-copy class.
- **Closing their measured instances only, as residual risk:**
  - foreign content is tracked against Chromium 156 only;
  - a raw-text start tag ignored in insertion modes other than template's column group (frameset is refused only for svg/math roots);
  - engines other than desktop Chromium. WebKit on phones was never measured, and lab E10 is desktop only;
  - the parser's own implied elements and reconstruction clones are bounded only by the measured gates, not modelled;
  - parse cost from inputs that are none of tags, attributes or formatting (the text-heavy gate was the measured worst at the historical depth 256, and is still the slowest gated shape in fix round 2's sweep at depth 64: 138 ms, **local**);
  - the `<html>`/`<body>` attribute merge, which review run 2259 found unmodelled: the parser copies a repeated `<html>` or `<body>` tag's new attributes onto the one element. At the historical limits (depth 256, 64 attributes) 255 `<html a0..a63>` tags were admitted and parsed in about 300 ms (**local**, the reviewer's reading). At the current limits a tag with 64 attributes is refused `too-many-attributes`, and the repeats are bounded by the depth limit, 64 since D-8695. It is a cost class, not a bypass;
  - the gate margin: under concurrent load the reviewer read the text-heavy gate at a 1 096.6 ms median (**local**), over its 1 000 ms gate, and 71/71 on a re-run alone. No gate was changed (fix round 1's ruling).
- What the seventh review covered: Chromium's adjusted current node and integration points, MathML text integration points against annotation-xml, svg tag-name case adjustment, nested svg/math, breakouts and `font`, self-closing acknowledgement, CDATA after exits, svg in table, select and template, and the two fuzzers above. Earlier rounds covered:
  - tokenizer states (round 5: comments, bogus comments, `</`, script escapes, attribute states);
  - the adoption agency and implicit closes (rounds 4-6);
  - foster parenting, template and select (rounds 5-7).
- **Cost of the rule:** omitted end tags are retained up to the depth limit (at depth 64, 32 copies of `<div><p>text</div>` reach it and 21 lists whose items are left open; at the historical 256, 128 copies), and 17 inline svgs that each hold an `<a>` are refused (`MOCKUP_MAX_OPEN_FORMATTING` + 1, a committed row in `mockup-prescan.test.ts`; 33 at the historical 32).
- **Bounded parse evidence at the historical limits** (v7 plus the cap: depth 256, formatting 32, attributes 64; the current limits' readings are in fix round 2 below): Chromium 156.0.8078.4, build `chromium-1248`, headless Linux, at most 12 generated fixtures per run, medians of three, ms. What the tree reproduces is the gate: the browser file holds each at 1 000 ms or less. Every reading in this table is **local**: the v6 column was taken on code no longer in the tree, and the v7 + cap column is one run's readings (review run 2259 read 217 for formatting, 832 for reconstruction at 64 attributes and 601 for text-heavy on a loaded box).

| Gate | v6 (**local**) | v7 + cap (**local**) |
|---|---|---|
| formatting F=32 at 15 000 tags | 93.0 | 126.0 |
| deep 256, span fill | 8.7 | 9.0 |
| deep 256, `<p>` fill | 8.6 | 8.5 |
| nested tables | 17.8 | 18.2 |
| property test's worst shape | 91.0 | 101.9 |
| text-heavy (255 divs, `x<!---->` to 2 MiB) | 445.7 | 531.1 |
| reconstruction at 64 attributes per element | — | 389.1 |
| adoption agency at 64 attributes | — | 53.2 |
| control E13 (660 KiB nested divs, parsed directly; refused `too-deep`) | 12 874 | 17 047 |

- **Measurement history (local):**
  - At 20 000 tags, v1 admitted misnested `<b><div></b>` at 430 ms, so `MOCKUP_MAX_TAGS` is 15 000.
  - Reconstruction at 20 000 tags took 37-78 ms with 16 open elements and 119-234 ms with 32, so `MOCKUP_MAX_OPEN_FORMATTING` was fixed at 32 (historical: 16 since D-8695).
  - Attributes measured at 0, 16, 32, 64, 96 and 128 per element gave 75, 173, 227, 358, 491 and 667 ms, so `MOCKUP_MAX_ATTRIBUTES` was fixed at 64 (historical: 32 since D-8695's lowering, under D-8671).
- **K1 (M5.EX4)**, every committed `docs/**/*.html` of at most 2 MiB, admitted:

| File | Depth | Tags | Formatting | Most attributes on an element |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `docs/design/overlord/reference.html` | 12 | 825 | 0 | 7 |
| `2026-10-07-box-token-lifecycle-design.html` | 11 | 5 394 | 2 | 2 |
| `2026-10-11-design-system-audit.html` | 8 | 763 | 1 | 3 |
| `2026-11-06-chat-reading-mockup.html` | 17 | 1 391 | 1 | 4 |
| `2026-11-06-desk-nav-mockup.html` | 12 (v6: 91, **local**) | 1 500 | 1 | 5 |

  The 6.7 MB frames file is over the cap, so it is download-only. The K1 test prints depth, tags and formatting per file (`PRESCAN-DOCS-FILE`) and, since fix round 1, each file's most attributes on one element by jsdom's parser (`PRESCAN-DOCS-ATTRS`). It asserts that every one is within `MOCKUP_MAX_ATTRIBUTES` and that desk-nav-mockup.html stands at depth 12.
- **Oracle and property test:** the real-Chromium oracle agrees with the scan on every page of its corpus (the browser file). The property test (jsdom/parse5, a bound of 36 elements per tag) has a worst admitted ratio of 0.33.
- **Mutation rows:**
  - The pre-scan's guard rows were measured red by the implementer on copies outside the work tree, the guard's own test failing in each: v5 10, v6 8, v7 9 and the cap 5 (**local**: no fence row records them). The guards are the test files' named cases; review run 2259 re-ran four of them in the tree and each went red.
  - The census rows below were measured in the tree.

- `mockup/plan.ts` (L1), `ports.ts` and `policy.ts`.
- `DocImage`'s size pre-check asks the shared `mockupSizeBasis` on every page but a version page.

- CarryReceipt "Reader image 401 and planner residue", Task 6's part: the reader, the plain view and the planner share one pin rule and one size basis. The 401 retry stays open for Task 8.

**Task 7 — bounded loading, origins and policy (D-8660, D-8662).**
- `mockup/css.ts`, `load.ts` and `origins.ts`, plus `policy.ts`'s CSP, sandbox, guard and the derived `MOCKUP_MAX_SRCDOC_CHARS` (9 090 078).
- One review and two re-reviews (Opus · high) found:
  - the unbounded built page: 500 uses of a 100 KiB image built 68 285 665 chars on the old code (**local**). The committed row has 9 021 663 chars, with 66 inlined and 434 `skipped:'over-budget'`. Asks 446 and 447 ruled it.
  - origins the CSP could not carry;
  - nesting re-encoding;
  - a 75 s build from 900 spellings of one import (the committed P4 row prints its time: 337-362 ms here, and review run 2259 read 1 307-1 313 ms on a loaded box, all **local**);
  - SVG style and script;
  - `@namespace`;
  - byte-exact stylesheets;
  - the ruled cut (the first use past the cap and every later one);
  - a `css-depth` verdict for rows no use reaches.
- The W8a clause allows `default-src 'none'` at `src/docs/mockup/policy.ts` exactly. The literal census registers the SVG namespace URI and the CSP meta skeleton.
- All 43 rows plus a descending-sort row are red. The T7-N1 to N5 rows fail on the plan's named discriminators.

- Superseded since C2 (noted at D1, review run 2378's F2): `C1-T7-auth-to-missing` quotes the end's own return, which C2's fix
  round 2 M-c (`a6536db6f`) removed from `load.ts`, so the row can no longer be applied. Its mutation, an `auth` word turned
  into a `missing` verdict, is re-recorded on `now()`'s surviving line as `NDR-D1-FI2-AUTH-TO-MISSING` (D1's fence), red on
  the same test. The row above keeps C1's recording.

**Lab and M5 rows C1 owns** (as ruled in fix round 1: measured means a committed test in this tree reproduces it).
- Measured above: M5.7 (mockup half), M5.8, M5.9, M5.10, M5.11, M5.12, M5.16 (the DOMParser and `default-src 'none'` bullets), M5.21 (`parseMockupOrigin`, `mockupCsp`), M5.22's pre-scan half (each refusal's typed return, with zero parsers constructed), M5.EX4, M5.B8, and D-8662's typed `too-large` return.
- M5.EX3: the test gates each 2 MiB `import(` repetition at 250 ms, against the spec's 50 ms (the spec's figure stands); this box read 3.9 to 13.0 ms (**local**, the row prints it). Its script-text census check (`via: 'script-text'`) is C2's.
- E13: the control above. E4: `MOCKUP_MAX_SRCDOC_CHARS`'s derivation (9 090 078, below E4's 11.2 M).
- Blocked for C2: M5.22's rendering half (the refusal copy "Too complex to render — shown as source", Source, Download and no render control: nothing in `pwa/src` calls `parseMockup` or `buildMockupSrcdoc` until C2), D-8662's Download on a `too-large` page, E3 and E9 (real-browser request counts; a verbatim `MOCKUP_CSP` check is no CSP proof, Task 10), M5.EX3's script-text census check, M5.13-15, M5.19 (the shell meta stands from W8a: `shell-csp.test.ts`), M5.EX1, EX2, EX5-EX7, M5.B1-B4, B7, B10, B11, E1, E5-E8, E10-E12, and labs 1-4, 6, 7, 10.
- Carried to C2: `isMockupOrigin`'s host grammar, which `mockupCsp`'s guard asks, accepts digit-only labels, so only `parseMockupOrigin` refuses an IP literal, `localhost` and the console's own host. A docs-scans clause (C1 origins) now holds that no type assertion names `HttpsOrigin` (bare, in an array, readonly array, `Array<>` or union, after `as unknown` too) except one inside `parseMockupOrigin`; a type alias, a structural literal of the brand and `any` are not seen (its NOT EXHAUSTIVE note). C2's caller must reach `mockupCsp` through it.

**Fix round 1 (review run 2259 at `2ef6fa23d`, 2026-11-16).** Six important findings and eight minors. The coordinator's rulings:
- F1 (planning quadratic in references, on untrusted input): fixed in this wave. The plan carries its basis (the page, whether it opens a draft, the listing indexed once) and each admitted pin's row index, and an extension derives only its new references (`0b5e7edc8`). `mockup-plan-linear.test.ts` counts one `resolveDocRef` per planned reference and a constant number of listing passes per plan. The review's page (190 000 uses of one image, then 63 stylesheets' references, a 100-entry listing) plans in 692-709 ms (**local**, three runs; the row prints it), where the reviewer measured about 0.8 s per extension and about 50 s in all. No row cap and no new limit.
- F2 and A2/A3: Results calls measured only what a committed test reproduces (the section above).
- F3, F5 (`d121ed9d0`): base §5.6.2 states the v7 rule in place, and D-8663 folds v3 to v7 (asks 448 and 450 to 454, found at the second review). `prescan.ts`'s header says the same.
- F4: §5.6.2's Status names what is enforced, the browser leg's 1 000 ms median gate on desktop Chromium 156, in place of "158 ms".
- F6: the figures no committed test reproduces are marked **local** above.

- Item 8 (the coordinator's addendum, mail 21828; D-8695, `039a748aa`). Merge-queue run 43905778054 dequeued PR #2915: in `browser (pwa) 2/3` at the `2ef6fa23d` content, reconstruction at 32 open elements with 64 attributes read medians 1 490.8, 1 410.4 and 1 341.3 ms (median 1 410.4), and text-heavy at depth 256 read 1 051.6, 1 223.9 and 1 037.6 ms (median 1 051.6), both over the 1 000 ms gate. The ruling: the gate stays and the limits come down, to at most 150 ms alone on the fleet box when quiet, keeping K1's 2x headroom. The current limits against the historical ones:

| Limit | Historical (v7 plus the cap) | Current | K1's maximum (committed test) | Headroom |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MOCKUP_MAX_DEPTH` | 256 | 64 (D-8695) | 17 | 3.8x |
| `MOCKUP_MAX_OPEN_FORMATTING` | 32 | 16 (D-8695) | 2 | 8x |
| `MOCKUP_MAX_ATTRIBUTES` | 64 | 32 (D-8671, with D-8695) | 7 | 4.6x |
| `MOCKUP_MAX_TAGS` | 15 000 | 15 000 | 5 394 | 2.8x |

  The sweep, alone on the fleet box (Chromium 156.0.8078.4, `chromium-1248`, headless Linux, the browser file's gate rows, medians of three, ms, all **local**; load averages 7 to 11):

| Depth, formatting, attributes | reconstruction | text-heavy | formatting | worst property | adoption | deep | tables |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 256, 32, 64 (historical) | 700.8 | 663.9 | 178.6 | 169.6 | 93.4 | 10.1 | 21.9 |
| 128, 16, 16 | 74.5 | 239.5 | 29.5 | — | — | — | — |
| 64, 32, 16 | 174.0 | 148.6 | 94.8 | 96.3 | 23.3 | — | — |
| 64, 16, 64 | 175.4 | 133.9 | 31.1 | 29.3 | 38.8 | — | — |
| 64, 16, 32 (chosen) | 108.0 | 138.1 | 31.1 | 34.1 | 20.5 | — | — |
| 64, 16, 16 | 68.8 | 132.3 | 29.3 | 28.0 | 16.8 | 3.1 | 5.3 |
| 48, 16, 16 | 70.9 | 130.5 | 29.6 | — | — | — | — |

  Text-heavy needs depth (it has a floor near 130 ms from the 2 MiB size), and reconstruction needs both formatting and attributes, so (64, 16, 32) is the smallest measured change that meets the target. One full run of the browser file at the chosen limits passed 71/71 on a loaded box (load 9.6, E13 control 30.9 s against 12.5 s quiet): reconstruction 307.9 ms, text-heavy 394.6 ms (**local**, about 2.5x slow by the control). A second full run at the head, at load 5.7 to 6.2 (E13 control 15.2 s), passed 71/71: reconstruction 205.8 ms, text-heavy 237.9 ms, formatting 74.1, worst property 80.4, adoption 32.0, deep 4.2, tables 7.6 (**local**). So the whole file reads the two slowest shapes above the sweep's figures, which ran only the gate rows. Text-heavy is at its depth floor, and a lower limit would not move it. These are local readings: they are not a hosted-runner pass or proof of the 2x hosted margin. That is the coordinator's full CI run. The first hosted medians (C2's fix round 1: the coordinator's full run 44049862947 on `b63b130df`, `browser (pwa) 2/3`, Chromium 157.0.8103.50 `build=usr`): reconstruction 278.0 ms and text-heavy 371.1 ms against the 1 000 ms gate, 3.6x and 2.7x, and every other gated shape at most 93.1 ms. Fix round 2 read them again, in the coordinator's full run 44066807476 on `a8d28d054`: reconstruction 321.6 ms and text-heavy 356.1 ms, 3.1x and 2.8x, and every other gated shape at most 84.6 ms.

- Minor 6 `58ec881d0`: the stale depth-256 test text corrected, and the `HttpsOrigin` census cites review run 2281 and plants the
  `ReadonlyArray<>` row its comment claimed.
- Minor 7 `58bbbbccf` and nit 2 `b9fe88c62`: the hosted margin. The parse gates' `M5B8-MEASURE` lines now print whenever the
  browser leg runs in CI (`CI=true` or `GITHUB_ACTIONS=true`, which GitHub Actions sets on every step), through one function,
  `browser/measure.ts`'s `printsGateMedians`; the 1 000 ms gate is unchanged. So every CI run of the browser leg, the
  coordinator's full run on this wave's tip included, carries the hosted medians in the browser shards' job logs; until that
  run is read, the 2x hosted margin stays unmeasured. It was read at fix round 1, 2.7x at worst, and again at fix round 2, 2.8x at worst (Task 10's fix round bullets), and on the tip that merged, 2.9x at worst (recorded at D1). A local run with `CI=true` printed the engine line, the eight gates and
  the E13 control's line (`58bbbbccf`'s message says "all nine gates": the file has eight `gate()` calls, and the ninth line is the
  control's report; corrected here, review run 2361's M6) and passed 71/71 (Chromium 156.0.8078.4, `chromium-1248`, headless Linux, load 7.6; **local** medians, ms: reconstruction 107.1,
  text-heavy 140.5, formatting 31.6, worst property 31.2, adoption 22.4, tables 5.4, deep 2.9 and 2.9; the E13 control 12 774).

**Task 8 — the mockup viewer and the reader's images (D-8711, D-8712).** Commits `824237046` (code and unit tests),
`6c8a927ad` (the real-browser rows) and the review fix round below.
- `MockupView.tsx` renders the html leaf in place of the stand-in: the pre-scan's refusal (its reason, counts and limits, Source
  and Download, no render control, zero parsers constructed), the 1 MiB tap gate, one parse, one attempt per context (plan,
  bounded load, build) with its own controller and generation, the frame, Run/Stop scripts, Load external (only origins
  `parseMockupOrigin` accepts with the console's host, never stored), Full screen and New tab through the console's
  `frame=full` shell, Source on one highlight store (plain first), the leaf's Download, the census and the links list, and the
  second-load notice that never remounts by itself. The class cap is download-only.
- `MockupFrame.tsx` renders the one iframe: `sandbox=""` or exactly `allow-scripts`, no `src`/`name`/`allow`/`allowfullscreen`,
  `no-referrer`, its `csp` attribute read back from the page's own leading meta (equal by construction; a page without that meta
  throws, so no frame renders without it), keyed by mode and nonce, and a new element for every published build.
- The view owns the one automatic whole-view re-list (D-8660): an observing reader reports a movement word; the first aborts the
  attempt, shows the changing note with Retry and asks the visit's re-list once; the next context plans afresh; a later movement
  only shows the note; Retry re-lists and the next context reads; a version page never re-lists; an `auth` row reads again after
  sign-in.
- The reader frame: images that answered 401 are counted and the body runs again once sign-in returns (the old mount's bar
  withdrawn meanwhile); an image ref over the library's 2048-unit seed limit (`READER_IMAGE_REF_MAX_UNITS`, pinned equal to
  `SEED_LIMITS.imageRefMaxCodeUnits`) is left out instead of sending the page to the plain view; an old body still cannot publish
  into a new one (an existing guard, now pinned). CarryReceipt "Reader image 401 and planner residue": fixed.
- The viewer's decisions (the scripts detector, the origin offer, the census rows and sentences, the links list, the 120-unit ref
  cut `MOCKUP_CENSUS_REF_UNITS`) live in `mockup/census.ts`, an L1 module by its imports; the view only acts on its answers.
- The canvas is a token: the frame's holder carries `data-theme="light"`, so `--mockup-canvas` (`var(--bg-surface)`) is the light
  palette's white surface in every theme (tokens only, base §4.14).
- Scans: clause W8a allows the `iframe` element and the `srcdoc` name at `src/docs/MockupFrame.tsx` exactly, with planted controls
  in `MockupView.tsx` and a sibling path; M4.EX16's homes gain it. `reader-lab-scans`' rule 3 (no sandbox token beyond
  `allow-scripts` in `pwa/src`) holds the new files.
- Files outside Task 8's Create/Modify lists, each as the coordinator ruled (mails 22432, 22476): `mockup/policy.ts` gains
  `MOCKUP_AUTO_RENDER_BYTES` (1 048 576, defined once, pinned, both boundary rows), `MOCKUP_CENSUS_REF_UNITS` and
  `MOCKUP_CENSUS_ROW_CAP`; the new `mockup/census.ts` and its test; `mockup/ports.ts`'s `refused.why` narrowed from `string` to
  the closed `MockupRefusedWhy` (type only) so every refused reason has a sentence; two test fakes (`docs-one-bar`,
  `docs-version`) take the new `auth: 0`.
- Words: the spec's viewer sentences are verbatim; the silences are filled in `docsText.ts`'s register as one block
  (`MOCKUP_TEXT`, `MOCKUP_REFUSAL_SENTENCE`, `MOCKUP_VERDICT_SENTENCE`, `MOCKUP_FAILED_SENTENCE`), each stating only what the code
  does (coordinator, mail 22432).
- The one-relist cap is per mounted view (review advisory A8, accepted as the coordinator conditioned it): each page edit makes a
  new view only because the view is keyed by the document's identity (a draft by its fingerprint), which a screen test holds
  (`8c3267bf0`); inside one view the plan's rows hold (a second movement shows the changing note with Retry and starts no third
  attempt). A continuously edited draft therefore re-lists at most once per real edit.
- Review (Opus · high, of `824237046`, read from git objects): two important findings, six minors and two advisories; the
  coordinator accepted the handling (mail 22476). One commit per finding:
  - I1 `c4954d0a6`: a build published over a showing frame changed the same iframe's `srcdoc`, which a browser reloads in place,
    and that second load read as the page navigating away. Each published build now gets a new frame element.
  - I2 `8111e7cf2`: the viewer's decisions move to `mockup/census.ts` (above); `pwa-rings.test.ts` pins it L1 (a planted
    `fetch(` turns the pin red).
  - M3 `b4fd61371`: the draft count is taken only for a build that renders. M4 `0b1360197`: the first movement shows the changing
    note with Retry at once, so a re-list that fails never leaves the view on Loading. M5 `dc97ee498`: Retry only re-lists; the
    next context starts the one attempt. M6 `b73d2e404`: the gate reads "Render mockup · N KiB", as base §5.6.2 step 3 says.
  - M7: (a) the rows are in the fence below; (b) the real-browser file is `6c8a927ad`; (c) `8c3267bf0`, three screen tests hold
    the leaf's key (the view, the frame and the draft fingerprint); (d) `ce772572f`, a test holds the sign-in `setBar(null)`.
  - A8: accepted as the coordinator conditioned it (the last bullet above). A9 `6c5a91238`: the refusal sentences are keyed over
    the pre-scan's own `why` union, `refused.why` is the closed `MockupRefusedWhy` with no raw-word fallback, and
    `mockup-frame.test.tsx` holds the `csp` read-back's unescape and its throw.
  - The folds' cap `52290aaf7` (D-8712, the coordinator's option (b), mail 22476): the pre-scan bounds tags, not `srcset`
    candidates, so a hostile page can carry about 100 000 references, and a fold that drew a row for each would freeze the
    console on one tap. Each on-screen fold, the census and the links list, draws at most `MOCKUP_CENSUS_ROW_CAP` (500) rows and
    then one closed line, "{n} more not listed"; the summary counts stay totals over every reference. The saved Export's links
    table stays uncapped: Task 9's final-bytes cap bounds it, and nothing here measured otherwise.
- Mutation rows, each in the tree, sequential, restored from a saved copy and its hash checked. The plan's named rows: sandbox
  `NDR-C2-T8-SANDBOX-STATIC` and `-SANDBOX-SCRIPTED-WIDENED`; second-load teardown `-SECOND-LOAD-TEARDOWN`; the meta-only control
  `-CSP-ATTR-DIFFERS` (the frame's `csp` attribute must equal the page's meta) and `NDR-C2-T8FR-A9-THROW` (no meta, no frame),
  with the browser's meta-only row in Task 10's map; the source-store path `-SOURCE-LANG` and `-SOURCE-NO-CODEBLOCK`; the draft
  state reset `-DRAFT-GIVE-BACK` and `NDR-C2-T8FR-M3-DRAFT-COUNT`; the auth retry `-RELIST-SIGNIN-RETRY-REMOVED` (the viewer) and
  `-AUTH-RETRY` (the reader frame); the stale body `-STALE-BODY` (the reader frame); the view generation guard
  `-GENERATION-CHECK` and `-RELIST-REPORT-GENERATION`; the disposal of P's buffered discoveries and results `-RELIST-NO-ABORT` (a
  held P stylesheet resolved after Q starts reaches nobody) and `-ABORT-ON-CLEANUP`; Q's fresh plan `-RELIST-Q-FROM-P-CONTEXT`;
  the once-only relist cap `-RELIST-CAP-REMOVED`. The reader frame's auth retry and stale body are `reader-frame` reds, not
  `mockup-view` ones, because `ReaderFrame.tsx` owns them; the viewer's own sign-in retry is a `mockup-view` red.

- Retired at fix round 2 (review run 2372's M-g): `NDR-C2-T8FR-M7D-SETBAR`. Fix round 1 re-pointed it onto the key's removal
  (`<ReaderBody key={signedIn} {...p}` to `<ReaderBody {...p}`), which is exactly `NDR-C2-FR1-F1-BODY-KEY`'s mutation, so two rows
  measured one mutation, and its red came from "no second run", not from the bar standing. Since F1 keyed the body, the bar has
  no code site of its own in `ReaderFrame.tsx` (each body's state, the bar included, starts over by construction), so no
  mutation of its own is left to re-point it to. The row stays above as the record of what it measured and is not replayed;
  its test (sign-in withdraws the old mount's bar until the new mount is ready) stays, and `NDR-C2-FR1-F1-BODY-KEY` measures the
  key.
- Superseded since D1's first item (F1 `ac6d4fa3a`, noted at D1): `NDR-C2-T8-ABORT-ON-CLEANUP` and `-RELIST-NO-ABORT` quote
  lines F1 split (it added the sign-in listener's drop beside each abort), and `-RELIST-SIGNIN-RETRY-REMOVED` quotes the
  `loaded`-keyed sign-in effect F1 replaced, so none can be applied. Each mutation is re-recorded on the current lines as
  `NDR-D1-RP-ABORT-ON-CLEANUP`, `-RELIST-NO-ABORT` and `-SIGNIN-RETRY` (D1's fence), red on the same tests. The rows above
  keep C2's recording.

**Task 9 — the static mockup Export.** Commits `9fa6330bd` (code and unit tests), `a92ae9fe8` (the real-browser rows) and the
review fix round below.
- `mockup/exportMockup.ts`'s `buildMockupSnapshot` clones the viewer's parsed inert Document (no second parser) and runs base
  §5.6.9's strip; the builder's mockup arm wraps it in the existing React shell with one `iframe sandbox=""`, the cap measured on
  the final escaped file; Save is the existing synchronous `downloadExport`, behind the draft confirm when draft files are inlined.
- `load.ts` exports its own `parseSrcset` and `base64Of` (two keywords, no behaviour change; coordinator, mail 22461), so the
  snapshot reads `srcset` with the one grammar and encodes with the one helper. `DocImage.tsx` keeps its own private base64 loop,
  which predates C1: a known duplicate for a later wave.
- Process: the wiring's tests were written after its code (the snapshot's and the timed-out mapping's were written first), a
  process note and no deviation (review run 2361's M3). Each test is shown discriminating by its measured mutation row, which is
  the evidence, not a red-first record: `NDR-C2-T9-FRAME-SANDBOX-OMITTED`, `-SIZE-BEFORE-ESCAPING`, `-DRAFT-CONFIRM-SKIPPED`,
  `-GATE-WORD` and `-VIEW-PUBLISH-DROPPED`, and `NDR-C2-T9FR-DRAFT-COUNT`, `-WITHDRAW-ON-MOVE` and `-CLEANUP-WITHDRAW`, each red
  in the fences and replayed red by review run 2361 at `b63b130df`; and `NDR-C2-T9-WAIT-NOT-TIMED-OUT`, which fix round 1
  re-recorded on its new line (`now(TIMED_OUT)` to `now('ended')`), a mutation review run 2361 did not replay: review run 2372
  replayed it red at `a8d28d054` (corrected at fix round 2, review run 2372's M-g). Fix round 2 replays all nine at its own tip.
- Scans: M4.EX2's scope gains `exportMockup.ts`; M4.EX16's homes gain `buildExport.tsx`'s `iframe` and `srcDoc`; clause W8a allows
  the `srcdoc` STRING at `exportMockup.ts` exactly (its strip list names the attribute to drop it), each with planted controls.
- M5.B10's reading (the browser rows): "in each, a click on a former link fires no second load" is asserted on the two builds whose
  links are stripped (all layers, strip only); in the CSP-only build the link is not stripped, so its click navigates, which is the
  self-navigation the row excludes by name.
- Review (Opus · high, of `9fa6330bd`, read from git objects): two important findings and six minors; the coordinator accepted
  the handling (mail 22505). One commit per finding:
  - I1 `41762f5ee`, with `e90224679`: the snapshot listed a file as omitted for each use it cut, so a draft image written three
    times and cut twice counted no draft and Save skipped the confirm (M5.EX5). `omitted` now lists an asset only when none of
    its uses was written, a cut stylesheet's nested files included and kept at its place in the list; the builder derives the
    inlined list and the draft count from the files it handed in minus `omitted`.
  - I2 (the browser file was missing from the reviewed commit): `a92ae9fe8`.
  - Minor 3 `1afd80fcb`: Export waits ("Preparing…") while the view re-lists. Minor 4 `87a487c7e`: a kept `data:` stylesheet
    link's `imagesrcset` follows `srcset`'s rule, and `attributionsrc` is always dropped.
  - Minor 5 `db9f9ed23`: the snapshot keys files by the pins the plan's rows carry. Its header records why its inliner is
    legitimately different from `buildMockupSrcdoc`'s (the coordinator's condition, mail 22505): another cap, the final escaped
    file's bytes rather than the frame string's characters; a strip that neutralises references rather than leaving them as
    written; and a stripped DOM clone as its output, not a string. What they share (`extractMockupRefs`, `parseSrcset`,
    `base64Of`, `css.ts`) is imported.
  - Minor 5, second half, `6a4093504`: the banner and the draft confirm read one worktree label. `exportWorktreeLabel` moves from
    `exportInput.ts` into `provenance.ts`, a file outside Task 9's Modify list (coordinator, mail 22505), which `exportInput.ts`
    and the sheet already imported: no new edge and no cycle. `share.ts` still makes the same choice for its own words: a known
    duplicate outside this wave's files.
  - Minor 6 `da5e206d7`, then `167627269`: the provenance says "<m> diagrams appear as source" (base §5.6.9's table). The count is
    `mockupDiagramCount(doc)`, its own function, so `buildMockupSnapshot` keeps the plan's interface exactly (`html`, `links`,
    `omitted`), which a test holds.
  - Minor 7 `55f7ee209`: the size cut keeps assets in document order (first use, depth-first, a stylesheet's nested files right
    after it). Minor 8 `403f67598`: the five guards that had no red test each have one.
- Mutation rows, each in the tree, sequential, restored from a saved copy and its hash checked. The plan's named rows: omit
  sandbox `NDR-C2-T9-FRAME-SANDBOX-OMITTED`; retain script, `on*` or an external URL `-STRIP-SCRIPT`, `-STRIP-ON-ATTR`,
  `-STRIP-URL-ALLOWLIST`, `-CSS-URL-KEPT` and `-CSS-IMPORT-KEPT`; omit the draft warning `-DRAFT-CONFIRM-SKIPPED` and
  `NDR-C2-T9FR-DRAFT-COUNT`; the pre-escape size test `-SIZE-BEFORE-ESCAPING`; an unsafe MIME `-MIME-FROM-ENTRY`; a second parser
  `-SECOND-PARSER` (the snapshot re-parses the page instead of cloning it: clause C1's census names the new constructor).
