**Task 10 — closing C2.**
- Allowances, each with its own planted evasion control in `docs-scans.test.ts`: clause W8a's `iframe` and `srcdoc` at
  `src/docs/MockupFrame.tsx` exactly (controls in `MockupView.tsx` and a sibling path), its `srcdoc` string at
  `src/docs/mockup/exportMockup.ts` exactly, M4.EX16's homes (`MockupFrame.tsx`, and `buildExport.tsx`'s `iframe` and `srcDoc`),
  and M4.EX2's scope widened to `exportMockup.ts`. W8a's three worker allowances are unchanged. No ring or scan exemption.
- The mutation rows of Tasks 8 and 9 (the fences above) were all replayed at `167627269`, the last code commit (`bce46303f`
  changes only a browser file no row runs): 118 rows, every one red, restored, its sha256 equal to the tip's file; the 119th,
  `NDR-C2-T9-SECOND-PARSER`, was measured at `bce46303f` after the browser suite, red and restored the same way. Seven rows
  were re-pointed for the replay because the fix rounds moved their code: `NDR-C2-T8-GENERATION-CHECK`, `-ABORT-ON-CLEANUP`,
  `-RELIST-NO-ABORT`, `-RELIST-CHANGING-NOTE-REMOVED`, `-RELIST-RETRY-NO-RELIST` and `NDR-C2-T9-BYTE-BOUND-OFF` (the same guard
  at a new snippet), and `NDR-C2-T8-DRAFT-DISTINCT`, whose decision moved into `census.ts` with I2 (its test is now
  `mockup-census`). Each row's counts are the replay's.
- The synthetic CDN-module diagram fixture (desktop Chromium, `docs-mockup.browser.test.ts`): two `pre class="diagram"`
  blocks drawn by a module imported from an origin the page does not ship. Under the base CSP the import is blocked
  (`TypeError`) and both diagrams stay as source; under `mockupCsp([that origin])` the module loads and both draw. Nothing
  broadens the CSP: the default stays source, and Load external is the only way to the drawn form. The released-build phone
  stage is Task 17's.
- E10 on desktop Chromium: through a 3 s busy loop in a scripted frame (3 000 ms measured), the parent's 50 ms timer's largest gap
  was 50 ms over 62 samples (the bound is 250 ms; the design measured 52 ms); the same loop in the parent showed a 3 007 ms gap
  (**local**, printed by the row). The phone half is Task 17's.
- Suites (`ccrc test`, foreground; the browser suite in the background, each shard under its own `timeout`, because CI
  measured one of its files at about 9.5 minutes, `slowFirst.ts`'s `FILE_MS`, past a foreground call's limit):
  - the whole pwa suite in eight shards, 554 files, 17 143 tests passed and 2 skipped. Shards 1 and 2 ran at `167627269`;
    shard 3 failed there on `browser-dist.test.ts` (the export browser file served the shared build as
    `serveDist(sharedDist())` in one call, not in the two-line shape the pin reads), fixed in `bce46303f`; shards 3 to 8 ran at
    `bce46303f`, all green;
  - pwa `tsc --noEmit` rc 0 (it covers `src`, `test` and `browser`);
  - the server guards that read this change (rings, single-definition, deviation-refs after a fetch, anchors, citations,
    growth-caps, growth-check, the consolidation census and path guard, ci-ledger-guard, ledger-crosstree, ownership, dtbd,
    oss-metadata, public-text, topology-clean, docs-parity, census-w7, census-kernel, no-default-saving-keystroke,
    crossrepo-prose): 66 files, 2 087 passed, 3 skipped;
  - agent: no file under `agent/` or `shared/` changes, and no agent test reads `pwa/` or `docs/` (their mentions are comments),
    so none ran here; the exact-tip CI run includes the agent tests for this change;
  - the whole browser suite in CI's three balanced shards: 102 files, 3 709 tests passed, 52 skipped and 1 expected failure, all
    green at `bce46303f` (shards of 34 files each, 563 s, 609 s and 481 s); the skips and the expected failure are `frame-cost`'s
    and `ask-inline`'s, files this branch does not touch, and the three mockup files (`docs-mockup`, `docs-mockup-export`,
    `mockup-parse`) hold no skip.
- Open for Task 17, not claimed here: every released-build and device row, among them the CDN fixture's phone stage, E10 on a
  phone, Save on iOS and Android and the export file opened there, and any engine but desktop Chromium (Firefox, WebKit and
  Android Chrome are unmeasured, as base §5.0 says). The parse gates' hosted margin (the first item's minor 7) is no longer
  open: fix round 1 read it in the coordinator's full run, 2.7x at worst, and fix round 2 in the next one, 2.8x at worst (below).

- The wave-done round (the coordinator, mail 22598, at `85e77e0ea`): two code-scanning alerts were open on the pull request's
  merge ref, 299 and 300, `js/bad-code-sanitization`, both in `docs-mockup.browser.test.ts`.
  - `f6e4f227e`: M5.B2's script vector and the real viewer's second-load page spliced their target URL into inline script source
    through `JSON.stringify`. Each now writes the URL into a hidden element's `data-href` attribute and the script reads it there,
    so the script text is a fixed literal. Test-only; nothing in `pwa/src` changes. Both rows stay green with their positives.
  - `961165e2f`: the coordinator's condition (every test using those pages stays green) covers M5.B2's click and api rows, which
    render the same `navHtml` pages, and their controls were flaky before the alert fix too: 2 of 6 runs failed at `85e77e0ea` and
    4 of 7 with the alert fix, interleaved under the same load. A copy that logged the frame after the click showed, in every
    failing run, the frame still on `about:srcdoc`, loaded and with its link, and nothing navigating: the click was lost. Most
    likely it arrived before Chrome routed input to the srcdoc frame's new process; that cause is not measured. The click is now
    repeated, at most five times, until the frame leaves `about:srcdoc`; five clicks with no navigation throw. Measured: the M5.B2
    rows green 5 of 5 at load 7 to 9 and the whole file 22 of 22; in 5 more runs that logged the attempts every first click
    navigated, so the loss did not recur there; a copy whose first attempt sends no click needed two attempts in the control and
    the production half, both green. The positives are unchanged. Its own commit, so the coordinator can strike it.
  - Lab 10 is open, routed to D (Task 13) by the coordinator (the row map below).
  - Run for this round: the whole `docs-mockup` browser file 22 of 22, the M5.B2 rows as above, pwa `tsc --noEmit` rc 0, and
    `browser-leg`, `browser-dist`, `docs-scans` and `reader-lab-scans`, 246 passed. The open alert count at the new head is the
    coordinator's condition and is reported in the fresh wave-done.
- Fix round 1 (review run 2361 at `b63b130df`, the coordinator's mail 22714), one commit per finding:
  - F1 `2acc752c1`: after sign-in the reader body's effect ran again while its reducer stood at `ready`, and `readerBodyReducer`
    takes `plain` only from `rendering`, so a second render that timed out, was refused or rejected left an empty host, and one
    still rendering showed no "Formatting…" note. `ReaderFrame` now keys the body by the count of those sign-ins, so the run after
    sign-in is a new body that starts at `rendering`. Keyed rather than reset: the reducer's rules stay true, and every per-body
    state, the bar included, starts over by construction (the explicit `setBar(null)` goes, and `NDR-C2-T8FR-M7D-SETBAR` is
    re-pointed onto the key). Four new cases (a second render that times out, is refused, rejects, and one still rendering)
    failed against the old code.
  - F2 `76e564471`, the coordinator's ruling (implement base decision 3 and §4.17's html row: wait up to
    `DOCS_EXPORT_IMAGE_WAIT_MS`, then placeholders for what is still loading). Export reads the view's own attempt, whose load
    answered only at its end, so at the timeout every admitted asset became a placeholder. The loader's started form,
    `startMockupLoad`, answers `now(word)`, the load as it stands, and leaves it running: every row whose read completed as the
    end would answer it, every admitted row still open failed with the word, the assets copied. It reads only state the loader
    keeps; `loadMockupAssets` is that load's end, unchanged for its callers. The view publishes `now` with the plan and the end,
    and Export calls it with `timed-out`. Edits outside Task 9's lists: `mockup/load.ts` gains `MockupLoad` and
    `startMockupLoad`, and `exportBody.ts`'s `MockupExportAssets` gains `now`. `docs-export.test.tsx`'s "omits every admitted
    asset" pin is replaced (two of three loaded save inlined and the third is worded as still loading; nothing loaded keeps every
    asset still loading), and `NDR-C2-T9-WAIT-NOT-TIMED-OUT` is re-recorded on the new line.
  - F3 `5a414bfe1`, D-8712 widened in place: the Export sheet's list of the assets left out and the saved file's, which is drawn
    again on every size-fit try, each draw `MOCKUP_CENSUS_ROW_CAP` rows, then "{n} more not listed"; the result keeps every label,
    and every placeholder stays. Boundary cases at 500 and 501 for each list.
  - M1 `ff0c49159`: `title` and `alt` are left out of the `url(` strip (`TEXT_ATTRIBUTES`), so a link target written into `title`,
    and an author's title or alt that mentions `url(`, keep their text; a `style` and an SVG paint attribute are still neutralised.
  - M2 `d8ac7a6c5`: the frame's nonce follows the page the build makes, adjusted while rendering, so a re-list whose build is
    byte-identical keeps the element (a frame running scripts keeps its state), and a changed page still gets a new element in the
    render that brings it (review I1; `NDR-C2-T8FR-I1-NONCE` re-pointed onto that line). The key stays mode and nonce. The attempt
    itself still runs again on a re-list.
  - M3 `9472f144a`: the process note under Task 9 names the rows that show each wiring test discriminates (no number).
  - M4 `7cc68f45b`: base §5.6.7's Census, §5.6.6's Links list and §5.6.9 step 1 name D-8712 in place, and this plan's Task 8
    interface names D-8711.
  - M5 `42d484d72`: `mockupDiagramCount` runs the snapshot's own element drop on a clone of its own and counts what is left (a kept
    `link` excepted), so a `template` or a SMIL element on `href` that carries the class counts nothing. The tests put the class on
    the dropped element itself, and the two stacked doc blocks are one each.
  - M6 `b9d493ce4`: minor 7's bullet says the local run printed eight gates and the E13 control's line (`58bbbbccf`'s message says
    nine).
  - M7 `28b163059`: `NDR-C2-FI6-BARE-ONLY` is re-recorded at the current `docs-scans.test.ts`.
  - M8: no change (`share.ts`'s copy is in the known duplicates).
  - Mutation rows: this round's new rows are the fence below. Re-pointed in their own fences, because this round moved their code:
    `NDR-C2-T8FR-M7D-SETBAR` and `-I1-NONCE`, `NDR-C2-T9-WAIT-NOT-TIMED-OUT`, `-NO-CLONE` and `-SECOND-PARSER` (the same clone,
    now with its next line, because the diagram count clones too), and `NDR-C2-T9FR-DIAGRAM-COUNT` and `-DIAGRAM-DROPPED-EXCLUDED`
    (the count's new body). Every C2 row on a file this round changed was replayed at `7cc68f45b`, this round's code (the Results
    after it change no row's file): 99 rows, 7 of them re-pointed, each red, restored from a saved copy, its sha256 the tip's
    file, and the fences carry the replay's counts and hashes. With this round's 11 new rows and `NDR-C2-FI6-BARE-ONLY` (M7), 111
    C2 rows were measured at `7cc68f45b`; the files of the other 24 have not changed since their replay at `167627269`. C1's six
    rows on `load.ts` (`NDR-C1-T7-N1`, `-N3`, `-N4` and `-N5`, and `NDR-C1-FR2-F7-LOAD-ADMITTED` and `-WITHVERDICTS-ADMITTED`)
    replayed red too; C1's fence keeps C1's recording.
  - Suites: pwa: every test vitest relates to this round's nine changed source files, 215 files, 8 158 passed and 2 skipped; the
    scan and census files, 23 files, 909 passed; `tsc --noEmit` rc 0; the three mockup browser files in desktop Chromium
    (`chromium-1248`), 113 passed. The server guards that read this change, 65 files: 2 084 passed and 3 skipped, and
    `deviation-refs`, whose worker the shared test slice's memory cap killed while other runs held most of its 6 GB, passed alone
    (34 tests); the plan-text guards run again after this commit.
  - The hosted margin (the first item's minor 7; M5.B8), read in the coordinator's full run 44049862947 on `b63b130df`
    (`browser (pwa) 2/3`, Chromium 157.0.8103.50 `build=usr`; hosted medians, ms, against the unchanged 1 000 ms gate):
    reconstruction 278.0 and text-heavy 371.1, so 3.6x and 2.7x; formatting 93.1, worst property 69.6, adoption 53.1, tables 12.4,

- Fix round 2 (review run 2372 at `a8d28d054`, round 2 of 3; the coordinator's mail 22802), one commit per finding:
  - F1 `168007de1`: the reader body subscribed to `onAuthRegained` only after its images walk had answered, and
    `clearAuthLost` announces once, to the listeners already subscribed, so a sign-in that completed while a queued image load
    was still pending was missed and the frame's "not loaded" chips stayed. It predates fix round 1; Task 8's reader-assets row
    (a failed 401 is visible and retryable after sign-in) makes it this wave's. The body now subscribes before the walk and
    latches a sign-in made during it: a walk that ends with a 401 starts the new body at once, with no drafts counted and no
    mount of its own; after the walk a sign-in starts it as before; a walk with no 401 drops the listener. Nothing reads
    `isAuthLost` at attach time, so a 401 whose body names no verdict cannot loop. The case (a sign-in while a queued load is
    pending, then a walk that answers 401) failed against the old code; `NDR-C2-FR2-F1-WALK-LATCH` is red.
  - F2 `8b825b189`, the coordinator's ruling (follow base §5.6.9 step 4, and define no new number: 8713 stays unspent): fix
    round 1's M1 exemption (`TEXT_ATTRIBUTES`, `title` and `alt` left out of the `url(` strip) is reverted. Step 5's targets are
    taken off every `a` and `area` before step 3 (which drops any `href` that is not `data:`), the strip runs over every
    attribute holding `url(`, an author's `title` and `alt` included, and only then is each target written into its `title`. So
    an author's title or alt holding `url(...)` is neutralised as step 4 says, and the target keeps its real text, the one
    non-`data:` URL M5.EX1 allows. NO URL LEAVES gains a scheme-ful `url(https://…)` in an author's title and in an alt, and
    M1's case becomes F2's; both failed against the old code. `NDR-C2-FR2-F2-TITLE-STRIPPED`, `-ALT-STRIPPED` and
    `-TITLE-AFTER-STRIP` are red. `NDR-C2-FR1-M1-TEXT-ATTRIBUTES` is superseded (the line it quotes is gone). No spec annotation:
    the code now matches step 4 and M5.EX1.
  - F3 `4c9c0947d`: the frame key's whole-page compare had no case that a length-only compare failed. In jsdom a re-list whose
    one asset answers other bytes of the same size gets a new element. In Chromium (`docs-mockup.browser.test.ts`, the real
    built app, the freshness chip re-listing) identical files keep the same element, with the same page and no notice, and a
    same-size stylesheet change publishes a new element whose one load is its page, with no notice; its control runs first (a
    changed srcdoc written into the same element shows the notice, about 30 ms here) and bounds both windows. The length-only
    compare is red in both: `NDR-C2-FR2-F3-WHOLE-PAGE` (the element is kept) and `-WHOLE-PAGE-CHROMIUM` (the notice shows
    where a new element should).
  - M-a `2ae866664`: the headers of `buildExport.tsx`, `MockupView.tsx`, `exportBody.ts` and `load.ts` describe `now()` and
    `startMockupLoad`.
  - M-b `82ec8e091`: `loadMockupAssets` stays, because C1's recorded rows and tests address the loader by this name; its header
    says so, and that its callers are tests. The loader's contract moves to `startMockupLoad`.
  - M-c `a6536db6f`: the loader's end is `now()` with no row left open (`finish` resolves `now(NONE_OPEN)`), so one rule fails
    open rows. Fix round 1's NOW rows stay red, and so do the five C1 rows on `load.ts` this round replayed (named under its
    mutation rows below); C1's other rows on `load.ts` were not replayed. (Corrected at D1, review run 2378's F2: this
    sentence first read "C1's loader rows stay red".) C1's `NDR-C1-FR2-F7-LOAD-ADMITTED` quoted the end's own return, which
    is gone; `NDR-C2-FR2-MC-END-ADMITTED` re-records its mutation on the shared return, red on the same test. So did
    `C1-T7-auth-to-missing`, which this round did not name: D1 re-records it as `NDR-D1-FI2-AUTH-TO-MISSING` (D1's first item).
  - M-d `9102e3d33`: three states of a load read at Export's deadline, each red on a mutation of its own. A stylesheet that
    completed while an earlier one still loads is inlined with its own reference neutralised and listed as left out (that
    reference is not a row yet, and `now()`'s doc now says so): `NDR-C2-FR2-MD-UNFED-CHILD`. A draft that loaded beside a slow
    asset is inlined as a draft, so the sheet's first Save tap still asks: `-DRAFT-AT-DEADLINE`. The size cut on a partial
    result keeps the loaded files that fit in document order, cuts the next and leaves the slow one its own word:
    `-CUT-PARTIAL`.
  - M-e `645825619`: the 500/501 case checks that every image past the omitted list's 500th row keeps its placeholder alt in the
    inner document: `NDR-C2-FR2-ME-PLACEHOLDERS-PAST-CAP`.
  - M-f `c5b5debd2`: the second run's refused and rejected cases assert the fallback note is absent; a mutant that sends that
    outcome to the fallback passed the old cases (2 passed) and is red now (`NDR-C2-FR2-MF-PLAIN-NOT-FALLBACK`). A sign-in while
    the first mount is still in flight is covered: the new body mounts with the image and the late first mount is destroyed
    (`-LATE-MOUNT`).
  - M-g `a4f649cbb`: `NDR-C2-T8FR-M7D-SETBAR` is retired, with its reason, under Task 8's fence, and the M3 process note says
    which review replayed which row. The six rows whose test files fix round 1 changed are re-recorded below, with every other
    row this round replayed.

### D1 — run 2380 (the first item and Tasks 11-12), 2026-11-17

Base `e5dc8a747` (main with C2 #2969, merged as `62c1565d2`, and ledger #2983), branch `ws/docs-reader-d1`. D runs as two
serial runs (the coordinator's ledger decision of 2026-11-17): this is D1; Tasks 13 and 14 are D2's. Reserve 8716-8718,
issued to this run, with no departure defined at run-open. Every figure below is reproduced by a committed test unless it is
marked **local**. Mutation rows are measured in the tree, one at a time, from a saved copy outside the work tree, each child
under `prlimit --nproc=4096` (1 488 tasks under the user when the run began, **local**) and `timeout -k 10 600` (900 for a
browser row), restored and its sha256 checked.

**First item (review run 2378's findings on C2), one commit per finding.** The review's report was gone from its clips
directory when this run opened (the review child had been reclaimed); it was recovered from the review session's own record.
- F1 `ac6d4fa3a` (important): the mockup viewer subscribed to `onAuthRegained` only in an effect keyed on the published
  result, so it listened only after the whole load had ended. `clearAuthLost` announces once, to the listeners already
  subscribed, so a sign-in that completed while the load was still pending, after one asset had answered `auth`, was missed,
  and that row stayed failed. The viewer contract is that an `auth` row reads again after sign-in (Task 8's reader-assets row;
  the Review Focus's "401 sign-in/retry"). Each attempt now subscribes at its start and latches a sign-in made during its load,
  as the reader's body does since review 2372 F1: a result with a row failed with `auth` then starts ONE new attempt at once
  and builds nothing; after the load a sign-in starts it as before; a result with no `auth` row drops the listener, the first
  movement drops the aborted attempt's, and the attempt's cleanup drops it otherwise. Nothing reads `isAuthLost` at attach
  time, so an `auth` row cannot loop. The test file counts live listeners through a wrapper around the real announcer. Red
  first: the mid-load case and the pending attempt's listener count failed against the old code (`Tests  2 failed | 2 passed |
  83 skipped (87)`). Five rows are red: the latch, the result's drop, the movement's drop, the cleanup's drop and the watching
  arm.
- F3 `b4bb4e83f` (important; the review's advisories A1 and A2 are the same finding): the reader body's drop of its listener
  after a walk with no 401 had no test that failed without it. The test file counts live listeners the same way. A new case
  raises a sign-in after a walk with no 401 and finds no listener of the body and nothing restarted, and the case named
  "…the body mounts once and stops listening" now asserts that the listener it held during the walk is gone after it. Test
  only: the guard was already there. `NDR-D1-FI3-NO-401-UNWATCH` is red (2 failed).
- F2 `b926c3c42` (important): row `C1-T7-auth-to-missing`, Task 7's named auth→missing mutation, could no longer be applied, because its
  `old` line left `load.ts` in C2's M-c; and C2's M-c bullet said "C1's loader rows stay red" where that round replayed five.
  C1's fence now notes the row superseded, with its reason, and the M-c bullet names what was replayed, each correction saying
  so in place. The mutation is re-recorded on `now()`'s surviving line, as the review re-pointed it:
  `NDR-D1-FI2-AUTH-TO-MISSING` is red on the same test (`auth stays failed with its own word, never missing`, 1 failed, the
  count the review read). The guard itself was intact.
- Fourth item `f6d9fba35`: the coordinator's full run 44085330692 on C2's merged tip `0dc099d28` is recorded beside the two earlier
  readings in C2's Results (fix round 2's hosted-margin bullets, minor 7's sentence and the row map's M5.B8 row), each figure
  read from the job's own log: reconstruction 213.6 ms and text-heavy 339.6 ms, 4.7x and 2.9x under the 1 000 ms gate.
- Rows F1 moved: three of C2's Task 8 rows quote lines F1 changed (the cleanup's and the first movement's aborts, now beside
  the listener's drop, and the old sign-in effect). C2's fence notes them superseded, and their mutations are re-recorded on
  the current lines, each red on the same test: `NDR-D1-RP-ABORT-ON-CLEANUP`, `-RELIST-NO-ABORT` and `-SIGNIN-RETRY` (the
  retry now sits in the attempt's listener, its watching arm).

**First item's review (Sonnet · high, the four commits above).** No important finding. Minor 1 is fixed in `c3db9091b` (the
auth-row listener case signs in before it unmounts, so its name says what it checks, and it is red on the cleanup row too);
the nit in `ef6b7386d` (a comment on what stays shown when a latched sign-in discards a result). Minor 2 stays as recorded:
in the movement case, the listener count carries `NDR-D1-FI1-MOVED-UNWATCH`, and its "starts nothing" half is not red on its
own, because without the drop `watching` is still false.

**Task 11 — the bounded client line diff.**
- `reader/diff.ts`, ring L1: it imports only `utf8Bytes` (`shared/utf8.ts`) and `DOCS_MAX_DOC_BYTES` (`shared/docs.ts`).
  `reader-diff.test.ts` has 49 cases. Red first, against a stub that always answered `unavailable`: `Tests  42 failed | 7
  passed (49)`. The seven that passed were the constants, determinism, two refusal fixtures, the bytes fixture's own size,
  one parser case and the purity scan.
- The line model is markdown-it's, because the rendered `data-line` comes from its source map: `\r\n`, a lone `\r` and `\n`
  each end a line, and a final line ending starts no line. The real parser's `data-line` is checked against the diff's
  numbering for LF and CRLF documents, with and without a final newline, rendered with the reader's own options.
- The bounds, refused in this order: bytes (`utf8Bytes` against `DOCS_MAX_DOC_BYTES`; no new byte constant); lines
  (`READER_DIFF_MAX_LINES`, 20 000 a side); work (`READER_DIFF_MAX_WORK`, 2 000 000 units: every line comparison, the trims'
  included, every frontier advance, and every trace cell copied, the band of 2d + 1 cells a step); output
  (`READER_DIFF_MAX_MARKS`, 512, counting ranges and removals together). The spec's "at most 512 output ranges/removals" is
  read as one count, the stricter reading, and the brief's "512 output marks" names it so; `NDR-D1-T11-OUTPUT-SEPARATE`
  holds it.
- A middle with one side empty after the trims (a pure insertion or deletion) is answered as one range or one removal with
  no search, so a 20 000-line insertion is not refused for work. That is the exact answer, not a second algorithm.
- The oracle: 2 000 seeded random pairs against an LCS dynamic program in the test file. The uncovered after-lines form a
  common subsequence of LCS length, the shape rules hold, and the reverse diff agrees.
- The removal mark against the real library (`removedAfter` 0, and an empty after document) is pinned in Task 12's browser
  file with the actual frame: the library's checker and marker (`readChangeSet` and `placesOf` in `enhance/changes.js`) are
  not exported.
- Wall clock (**local**, vitest's per-test durations; no disk benchmark): 750 + 750 disjoint lines, refused for work, 6 ms;
  300 + 300, 5 ms; 513 isolated insertions, 3 ms; 20 000 identical lines, 5 ms; the bytes boundary (three diffs of 2 MiB
  sides), 15 ms; the 2 000-pair oracle, 98 ms.
- No row for the trims' comparisons: they are counted, but no fixture tells them apart from the search's units without a
  hook that reports the count, and the lines bound holds them under 40 000 units.
- Rows: 13, each red (the four bounds, the combined mark count, the line origin, CRLF, the final line ending, both removal
  origins, and each kind of work).

**Task 11's review (Opus · high, `6261d3ab3`).** No important finding. The algorithm was checked exhaustively by the
reviewer (every pair over a 3-letter alphabet up to length 6, 1 194 649 pairs, and 20 000 random pairs up to 80 lines: the
steps rebuild both sides and the kept count equals the LCS), and the bounds hold. Four minors and the nit are fixed, one
commit per finding:
- Minor 1 `da2437aad`: the `lines` refusal came after a full split (a 2 MiB text of line endings built a 2.1 M-element
  array first, about 55 ms in node, **local**, several times the whole budgeted search). The split is now cut at
  `READER_DIFF_MAX_LINES + 2` parts, so a cut result still holds more lines than the bound after the final-ending pop and
  the refusal stays exact. Red first: `Tests  1 failed | 49 passed (50)` (a spy on `String.prototype.split` saw an array of
  2 097 153 elements).
- Minor 2 `933068700`: the bytes check counted the whole string before refusing. A side whose `length` passes
  `DOCS_MAX_DOC_BYTES` is refused before `utf8Bytes` reads it (every UTF-16 unit is at least one UTF-8 byte, so this is
  exact). Red first: `Tests  1 failed | 50 passed (51)` (a pass-through wrapper saw the byte counter read 2 097 153 units).
- Minor 3 `d05b290b9` (test only): the oracle checked the shape of `removedAfter`, not where removals land. Optimal
  alignments can place removals differently, so the oracle now decides, with a dynamic program over (survivor, before
  line), whether SOME embedding of the survivors into the before lines has deletions in exactly the marked gaps, for all
  2 000 pairs both ways; two hand cases name the bugs it was blind to (a removal after the next survivor, two separated
  gaps given one removal).
- Minor 4 `ea2c6256a` (test only): each parser case ties the rendered `data-line` to `diff.ts`'s own line count (the
  largest rendered end equals it when the text ends in a block), with two lone-CR variants and an empty after.
- Nit `f80127264`: the header's tie sentence named the wrong move (a tie moves right, a deletion, as standard Myers does),
  and a case pins the order of `work` before `output` (a text over both answers `work`).
- Kept as recorded: the output cap is checked after the walk builds its arrays (they are bounded by the lines bound, about
  20 000 entries); there is no exact-boundary case for `work` (`>` against `>=`); the trims' count has no row (above).
- The fix round's two rows are red: `NDR-D1-T11FR-SPLIT-LIMIT` and `-LENGTH-PRECHECK`. Minor 2 put the length check in front
  of the byte count, so `NDR-D1-T11-BYTES-GUARD` is re-pointed onto the byte-count half (the same mutation: the bound no
  longer refuses a side whose bytes pass the cap), red at the tip with the other Task 11 rows.

**Task 12 — the history seed, the changes adapter and the version state.** Commit `51a3584be` (code and unit tests),
built in two parts by Sonnet · high implementers, each read before it was committed.
- `reader/history.ts`, ring L1 (imports only `shared/docs.ts`): the plan's `ReaderCommit`, `ReaderVersionSeed` and
  `readerVersionSeed` exactly. A served hash that is not a full commit is `null`, never cut into validity. The log is read
  by own properties only (an inherited field counts as missing): at most 100 commits (`READER_SEED_MAX_COMMITS`), subjects
  cut to 200 UTF-16 units without splitting a pair (ccd cuts subjects to 200 code points, so an astral subject can pass
  200 units), dates of at most 64 units, parents `[]` or `[parent]`, no author. A truncated walk, a missing log, or any
  malformed entry among the first 100 gives `commits: null`: the frame cannot be told a list is partial, so an incomplete
  list is never shown as the doc's history (the library marks a full list of 100 as "100+" itself). `only` is kept when it
  names an entry or an entry's parent, so the oldest day's baseline, which is no entry, is admitted. Beside it, two
  decisions the plan's interface leaves to the implementation: `readerVersionMove` (the version op: `at` from a full
  `sha`, the latest without one, the filter as given, a field that is not a full hash refusing the whole request; M8.22)
  and `readerChangesBase` (the base pin a `changes(sha)` reads: none for a draft; G5). The four seed limits equal the
  library's `SEED_LIMITS`, which it does not export; the browser row at the limits pins the frame's acceptance.
- `reader/policy.ts`: `readerMountPlan` gains `history` (`false` for the reader lab's `lab-draw`, `true` for the Docs
  reader), the one decision behind "lab mounts remain history-free"; its two whole-object pins in `reader-policy.test.ts`
  follow (a consequential edit, claimed).
- `reader/bridge.ts` stays the one runtime importer of the library's host. It passes the seed (the document's version is
  exactly the seed's four fields), `changes` and `openVersion` only when the plan carries history; otherwise
  `withServedVersion` cuts the document's own version as before. `openVersion` forwards a fresh object holding only the
  `sha` and `only` the library gave. `reader-history-types.test-d.ts` holds both callbacks equal to `HostAdapters` in both
  directions and `ReaderCommit` equal to the library's `CommitEntry`. The file's own full-hash regex gave way to
  `shared/docs.ts`'s `isDocsCommit`, the one owner of that check.
- `readerHistory.ts`, ring L4: one body's lifecycle, keyed to the body's abort signal. `startReaderHistory` reads the log
  with the opened committed pin (B2's `DocsHistoryLoader`), started with the render so it runs beside the render and the
  images, and the body awaits the seed before it mounts. `changes(sha)` reads exactly two committed pins, the opened one and
  `readerChangesBase`'s, through the screen's own queue and loaders (no new endpoint, cache, lane or verb), and answers
  `readerDiff`'s marks over the text the frame rendered (a head whose text differs from it answers `null`); a draft, a
  failure, no text, an overflow and an ended body answer `null`, never empty marks. `openVersion` navigates through
  `docsLocation` (the ref and frame kept) and records the day filter for the new entry. `useReaderOnly` reads the filter
  from the current history entry's `history.state`, writes a pending filter into the entry it was set for, follows every
  `popstate` (the router's `navigate` sends one even when the URL does not change), and survives a reload; the filter is
  never a URL key and never storage.
- `ReaderFrame.tsx` and `DocsLeaf.tsx`: the leaf hands the body its opened pin, its page and the entry's filter, and keys
  the frame by the filter, so a new filter on one URL is a remount that seeds it (M8.22). The document no longer carries
  its own version: the seed is the one carrier, so `reader-frame.test.tsx`'s two version reads and `docs-version.test.tsx`'s
  two (B2's) now read the mount input (consequential edits, claimed).
- `DocsScreen.tsx`: one line, `history: api.docs` in `DEFAULT_READER`, outside Task 12's Modify list by the coordinator's
  ruling on ask 517 (mail 23011): `ReaderDeps.history` is optional and typed as the existing `DocsHistoryLoader`; absent,
  history is unavailable (`log` null, so the seed has no commits; adapter case 12 pins it).
- Red first: `reader-history.test.ts` against a stub, `Tests  26 failed | 5 passed (31)`. `reader-history-adapter.test.tsx`
  was written before `readerHistory.ts` existed, so its first run failed as a whole file (the module did not resolve); its
  guards are measured by the rows below, not by a case-by-case red.
- One row first survived: `NDR-D1-T12-SEED-ENDED` (the seed's own check for an ended body), because `ReaderFrame` checks its
  abort again after the seed. Case 5b now pins the history body's contract on `startReaderHistory` itself (a body that ends
  while its log is held settles its seed at once, and never seeds when the log answers later), and the row is red.
- Suites at `51a3584be`: the related pwa suites (every `reader-`, `docs-` and `mockup-` test file, the four source
  censuses and `w7-single-readers`), 65 files, 2 887 passed; `tsc --noEmit` rc 0.
- The real-browser rows, `9d7cd000f`: `reader-history.browser.test.ts` drives desktop Chromium (`chromium-1248`, **local**)
  over the production build with the actual library frame and ccrc's own reader code; only `/api/docs/*` is answered from
  fixtures (the listing, each commit's file, the doc's history). Eleven rows, three runs in a row green (the last, 11
  passed in 32.7 s, **local**): the history read once at the opened pin and a day comparison marking exactly the blocks the
  real `readerDiff` names (`.chg` and `.chg-del`) with the stepper's count; `removedAfter` 0 accepted with no block marked for
  it (Task 11's pin with the real library); unavailable (a failed base read marks nothing and does not fall back); a draft
  (no trigger, no history read, only its own body read); Only this day with Back to the latest (`at` and the ref in the URL,
  the filter in `history.state`, never in the URL); back, forward and reload; last visit and prefs across visits under
  `ccrc:reader:v1:`, the visit the doc's own; blocked storage keeping reading usable; a late read after teardown marking
  nothing; and the seed at its limits (100 entries, 200-unit subjects, 64-unit dates) mounting ready with the menu. The
  rows run the production `DocsScreen`, whose default reader deps carry the history loader, so they also guard ask 517's
  one line (`NDR-D1-T12-SCREEN-HISTORY-CHROMIUM`).
- The empty after document (Task 11's other half) is measured on the diff's answer and on the mount, not on the frame's
  acceptance: the library builds its comparison menu, its day view and its last-visit notice only for a page that is a
  document (`isDocument` in `enhance/lifecycle.js`: a title and at least three sections), so for an empty head the frame
  never asks. The row asserts the real `readerDiff` answers `{ ranges: [], removedAfter: [0] }`, the page mounts ready with
  no fallback and no error, the history is read once, and the frame offers no trigger.
- The review's accepted items (Opus · high, `51a3584be`), recorded rather than changed:
  - the seed is awaited before the mount with no client time limit: the plan's "assemble optional seed before mount", and
    the docs loaders have no client limit by design (`pwa/src/lib/api.ts`: the server bounds them, ccd's docs verbs 15 to
    20 s after a read lane's wait of at most 10 s); the log read starts with the render, so it usually costs nothing extra;
  - the log read takes no signal (B2's `DocsHistoryLoader` has none) and every remount reads it again, a new filter's
    remount included; a superseded read runs until the server answers, and its answer is dropped;
  - a 401 on the log or on a `changes` read starts no sign-in restart (only the images walk's 401 does): the log then reads
    as unavailable and the comparison as unavailable until the next body; the window is the moment after the body's own
    file read answered with the same session;
  - a navigation that takes the router's full-page reload (`reloadOnMove`, after a release) drops a pending filter: the
    version opens with no filter, never with a wrong one;
  - a fallback does not abort a `changes` read in flight: its answer is dropped, and its queue slot frees when it settles.

**Task 12's review (Opus · high, `51a3584be`).** No correctness defect. Two important findings, both test gaps, are fixed,
and the minor and nits below, one commit per finding (Sonnet · high):
- Important 1 `9dcd26d45` (test only): the history read's failure mapping had no test. Without its `catch`, a rejecting
  loader made the seed reject, `ReaderFrame`'s run threw after the images, and nothing landed the body: the page stayed on
  "Formatting…". Case 1b mounts the real `ReaderFrame` over a failure word, an auth slot, an unreachable slot and a
  rejection, each with no commits and no filter; case 1c holds that `startReaderHistory`'s seed resolves for a rejecting
  loader. The `catch` stays the one guard (`ReaderFrame` adds none). Rows `NDR-D1-T12FR-CATCH` and `-NONOK-EMPTY-LOG`.
- Important 2: the one `DocsScreen` line had no guard, because every unit test injects its own deps. The browser rows run
  the production screen, so deleting the line reds them: `NDR-D1-T12-SCREEN-HISTORY-CHROMIUM`.
- Minor 3 `a6867ce19` (test only): a pending filter being spent once was not tested. Case 8b lands on a URL with a filter,
  pushes the same URL again as a new entry, and finds no filter there, while back finds the first entry's own:
  `NDR-D1-T12FR-PENDING-KEPT`.
- Nit 8 `cdfdad79c`: the served hash (a draft's head, else the commit) was decided in `readerHistory.ts` and in
  `ReaderFrame.tsx`; it is now `history.ts#readerServedHash`, the one decider, red first against a stub (`3 failed | 32
  passed`): `NDR-D1-T12FR-SERVED-DRAFT-COMMIT`.
- Nit 9 `712825fbb`: the decision to read the log sat in L4; it is now `history.ts#readerLogAsk` (none for a draft, a draft
  pin or a hash that is not a full commit), red first against a stub (`1 failed | 38 passed`): `NDR-D1-T12FR-LOGASK-DRAFT`.
  The header sentence naming both deciders landed in `cdfdad79c`, one commit early, and `712825fbb`'s message says it is
  there; no commit was rewritten.
- Nit 10 `b0934f5a2`: the pending filter is module state, so the adapter test clears it between cases with
  `resetReaderOnlyForTests`, after the `reset…ForTests` precedents in `lib/swupdate.ts` and `lib/character.ts`.
- Accepted rather than changed: the minors 4 to 7 and nit 11, recorded with the real-browser rows above.

**The close of D1 (Task 14's D1 half).**
- Departures: none found. The reserve 8716 to 8718 is unspent. What was decided inside the plan's own text is recorded where it
  was decided (the combined mark count, the history that is not supplied, the extra L1 deciders), and the one file outside
  Task 12's list is the coordinator's ruling on ask 517, which spent no number.
- A pin found before the first whole run: `browser-dist.test.ts` holds that every browser file serves the shared build as
  `dist = sharedDist()` and then `serveDist(dist)` (C2's fix round met the same pin); the history browser file served it in
  one expression. Red first (`Tests  1 failed | 11 passed (12)`), fixed in `ef4f4ce78`, before the suites below ran.
- Suites at `ef4f4ce78` (the code at `b0934f5a2`, plus that one test line):
  - the whole pwa unit suite in eight shards: 559 files, 17 347 passed and 2 skipped. Shards 1 and 2 first ran with the
    default workers, and the shared test slice's memory cap killed three files mid-run (`reader-lab-scans`, `docs-markdown`,
    `highlight-measure`: SIGKILL, no assertion failed); both shards ran again whole with four workers, green, as did shards 3
    to 8;
  - pwa `tsc --noEmit` rc 0 (it covers `src`, `test` and `browser`; run at `b0934f5a2`, and `ef4f4ce78` changed one test line);
  - the server guards that read this change, C2's set (rings, single-definition, census-w7, census-kernel, deviation-refs
    after a fetch, anchors, citations, growth-caps, growth-check, the consolidation census and path guard, ci-ledger-guard,
    ledger-crosstree, ownership, dtbd, oss-metadata, public-text, topology-clean, docs-parity, no-default-saving-keystroke,
    crossrepo-prose): 68 files, 2 125 passed and 3 skipped; the plan-text guards run again after this commit;
  - the whole browser suite in CI's three balanced shards at `ef4f4ce78` (desktop Chromium `chromium-1248`, **local**): 104
    files, 3 729 passed, 52 skipped and 1 expected failure (`frame-cost`'s, as in CI), with shards 1 and 3 green and one red
    in shard 2: `frame-cost.browser.test.ts`, two cases, each a `window-overrun`, the file's own loaded-box arm (a measurement
    window that passes its 2 000 ms gap plus 1 000 ms of slack fails the attempt and is never taken as a cost), at a load
    average about 20 with CPU pressure about 37 % (**local**). Run alone under the same load it overran again, on other cases.
    D1 changes no fleet code; CI's browser leg on the quiet runner is the arbiter. The history file itself ran green there;
  - agent: no file under `agent/` or `shared/` changes, and no agent test reads `pwa/` or `docs/`, so none ran here.
- The mutation table: every D1 row was replayed at the code tip `b0934f5a2`, one at a time, from a saved copy outside the work
  tree, under `prlimit --nproc=4096` and `timeout -k 10 600` (900 for a browser row), restored and its sha256 checked: 56
  rows, each red (the fences above carry the replay's counts and hashes). Every earlier row whose source file or test file D1
  changed, recorded red in this plan or in the W5, phone-header, reader-lab, take-up and W5b plans and still applying, was
  replayed the same way: 96 rows, each red (the fence below; W5's share rows `W5S-T4-M3` and `-T6-M3` record their one test
  file as a string, and run on that file). Three runs were killed by the shared slice's memory cap (exit 137, no counts) and
  ran again alone, red: a kill is not a red. Not replayed, and why: C1's `C1-T7-auth-to-missing` and C2's three Task 8 rows
  (superseded, re-recorded above), C2's retired `NDR-C2-T8FR-M7D-SETBAR` and superseded `NDR-C2-FR1-M1-TEXT-ATTRIBUTES`, and
  three W5b rows already unappliable at D1's base (`W5B-T17-M2` on `useDocsVisit.ts`, `W5B-T18-M5` and `-M6` on
  `DocsHead.tsx`, files D1 does not change; their lines moved before D1, so they are not D1's to re-point). The five history
  browser rows were measured at `b0934f5a2`, before `ef4f4ce78` changed only how that file takes the shared build's
  directory.

## Where things stand

### Agreed

- One remaining delivery line in this programme; reuse approved choices and both phone blocks.
- A includes real Settings access; B keeps its approved two-run split; W7 is last.
- Implementation, review, landing, release, installed evidence and retirement are distinct facts.

### Waiting on you

- Do not repeat approval/signing/phone evidence or the recorded frozen-snapshot choice. Required follow-on designs still owe access-model/comment/security decisions and any genuine parity/state conflict; raise only operator-owned choices as precise console items when needed. Reserved rollout, device and retirement actions also wait for exact entry evidence; all unblocked work proceeds autonomously.

### Later

- Only the design §7 explicit accepted/deferred/non-goal rows. No unowned “follow-up” may be used to erase a required delivery task.
